Keep it accurate; prune anything that becomes wrong.

## What this is

**Rustroom** is an open-source alternative to Adobe Lightroom (Classic model): a non-destructive RAW photo editor. **Linux first**, then Windows/macOS from one codebase.

- Ambition: a **serious, usable** tool on a focused feature set — not a toy, not a full LR clone.
- Focus order: **Develop (editing) first, Library (catalog/DAM) later.**
- **Reuse proven libraries** (libraw, LittleCMS, lensfun) rather than reimplementing decoding/color science.

## Stack

| Concern | Choice |
|---|---|
| Language | **Rust** (Cargo workspace) |
| RAW decode | **libraw** via bindings (swappable; `rawler` is the pure-Rust alt) |
| Color management | **LittleCMS** (`lcms2`) |
| Lens corrections | **lensfun** (later phase) |
| CPU parallelism | **`rayon`** |
| Image export I/O | **`image`** + jpeg/tiff/png crates |
| GUI | **`egui` + `wgpu`** (Slint reconsidered later) |
| Catalog DB (later) | **SQLite** via `rusqlite` |
| Edit persistence | sidecar **JSON** now → catalog DB later |

**Why Rust over C++/Qt:** cross-platform single codebase, native perf w/o GC, memory/thread safety, and — the deciding factor — `wgpu` is one GPU compute API across Vulkan/Metal/DX12/GL, making the later CPU→GPU port share one abstraction.

## Core architecture (the load-bearing decisions)

1. **Non-destructive.** Originals are never modified. An edit = a parameterized entry in an ordered **op-list**. Rendering replays the op-list. This gives history, virtual copies, and presets almost for free.
2. **Scene-referred, linear, float32 working space.** Math in linear light; display/output transform applied only at the end.
3. **Staged pipeline behind a `PipelineStage` trait.** Each stage is an independent, unit-testable `apply(image, params) -> image`. A stage's CPU impl can later be swapped for a `wgpu` compute shader **without changing callers.**
4. **Interactivity via previews.** Edit downscaled previews for instant feedback; render full-res only on export / 1:1 zoom.

Pipeline order:
```
decode → linearize/black-white → white balance → demosaic →
input color profile → working space (linear f32) →
geometry (crop/straighten) → noise reduction → exposure →
tone (highlights/shadows/curve) → color (HSL/grading) →
vignette → sharpen → output transform → encode
```

**Geometry runs first**, not last, so every adjustment acts on the final cropped pixels —
which is also what makes the vignette post-crop for free, with no separate mode. (Lens
corrections will join it there when `lensfun` lands.)

**NR early, sharpening late** — the two halves of "detail" belong at opposite ends. Denoising
the source is far easier than denoising after Exposure/Shadows/the tone curve have amplified
the grain, and it prevents HSL chroma speckle instead of smoothing it after the fact.
Sharpening is last because it must not amplify anything a later stage would then stretch.

## Planned crate layout (Cargo workspace)

```
opensource_lightroom/
├── Cargo.toml                # [workspace]
├── CLAUDE.md                 # this file
├── crates/
│   ├── core-image/           # ✅ ImageBuf (f32 RGB) + Capture (EXIF facts, both decoders) + map_samples/map_pixels/map_pixels_xy/to_rgb8/to_rgba8 (rayon-parallel) /crop/downscale_to + Thumbnail
│   ├── raw-decode/           # ✅ libraw(rsraw); DecodeOptions{linear_scene,display,preview(half_size),demosaic}; Demosaic tiers -> user_qual; extract_thumbnail
│   ├── image-decode/         # ✅ JPEG/PNG/TIFF → linear ImageBuf (image + srgb_to_linear) + thumbnail; EXIF orientation applied in both
│   ├── color/                # ✅ sRGB transfer fns + Oklab (lcms2/ICC still deferred)
│   ├── edit-model/           # ✅ serde Edits op-list (orientation/WB/exposure/contrast/tone/curve/saturation/hsl/local_contrast/dehaze/NR/sharpening/vignette/geometry) + EditGroups partition + merge_from
│   ├── pipeline/             # ✅ PipelineStage + 14 stages (incl. geometry, noise_reduction, dehaze, saturation, hsl, local_contrast, vignette, sharpening) + spatial::{box_blur, gaussian_blur, wide_blur, min_filter, guided_filter} + build_pipeline / build_pipeline_split(_in_region) / build_prefix_steps + PrefixStep/PrefixStepKey/PrefixKey + Region + tail_kernel_radius + golden tests
│   ├── export/               # ✅ to_rgb8 + save_jpeg (resize/sharpen later)
│   ├── rustroom/             # ✅ CLI crate; binary `rustroom` — `decode` (dump) + `render [--edits]` (RAW)
│   ├── app/                  # ✅ GUI `Rustroom` (eframe/egui 0.35 + wgpu). lib=history/action_log/edit_history/selection/crop_tool/histogram/render_engine/thumb_loader/thumb_cache/thumb_window/metadata_scanner/date_tree/library_filter{RatingSet,FlagSet,LabelSet}/decode_worker/buffer_cache/view_region/full_res/format_filter/presets/export_presets/safe_name/settings/edit_clipboard/sidecar/sync/batch_export/photo_id/entries/file_rename; bin-only modules=theme/modals/name_edit/develop{mod,panel,canvas}/library/filmstrip/thumb/shell (egui lives here, not in the lib)
│   └── catalog/              # ✅ scan_folder → RAW+JPEG/PNG/TIFF entries (+ mtime); SortOrder/sort (name/modified/edited, case-insensitive, name tie-break); is_raw/is_supported (in-memory; SQLite DAM later)
├── docs/
└── tests/                    # integration + golden reference images
```

Key rule: **`pipeline`, `color`, `export` have zero GUI dependency** and are driven headless by `cli` and tests.

## Conventions & engineering discipline

- **CI on Linux from day one:** `cargo build`, `cargo test`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo fmt --check` must stay green.
- **Golden-image regression tests** (established at plan milestone M1.2): render a fixed RAW with a fixed op-list, assert output matches a checked-in reference within tolerance. Never abandon these — they make the GPU port safe.
- **Keep crates small and single-purpose.** A file growing large signals it's doing too much.
- **Color correctness is first-class** — validate against references early.
- **Design the GPU seam now, build it later.** Don't leak CPU-specific types into the pipeline interface.
- Avoid platform-specific assumptions (paths, file dialogs, GPU backend) so Win/macOS stay cheap.

**⚠⚠ AND A NEW VARIANT, 2026-08-26: a broken sabotage HARNESS reported all seven guards "NOT CAUGHT".** The matcher looked for failure lines by bare test name while cargo prints them with module paths, so nothing ever matched. That is the mirror image of the finding below — a universal false *alarm* rather than a false pass — and both come from the same root: **trusting the harness's output shape instead of checking it produces what you grep for.** Check the harness against a sabotage you already know is caught.

**⚠⚠ A standing lesson, now ELEVEN instances deep: a test can name a bug it cannot detect.** Each time it looked rigorous and checked nothing. ⚠ **M3.1b alone produced three, all caught by the sabotage step and none by review** — which is the strongest argument yet that the step is not a formality.

**They share one shape: each asserted a property at inputs where the bug is inert.** That is the thing to check when writing the test, not after.

- **Rotate/flip**: "the generators reach exactly 8 states" passed against a *wrong abelian* composition rule — the count cannot tell a direct product from a dihedral group.
- **Masking 3.1a, `hit_test`**: `hit_testing_the_line_is_aspect_correct` used a horizontal axis with the probe directly below it, where the perpendicular distance is purely vertical — it passed with the aspect correction deleted.
- **Masking 3.1a, `tail_kernel_radius`**: the region-equality test was under-inset by ~9 px and passed because the residual differences happened to round the same way, until a masked gain pushed them over a quantisation boundary.
- **M3.1b, `feather_zero_is_a_hard_edge`**: probed either side of the ellipse boundary but never *on* it. Without the `FEATHER_EPS` guard the ramp is `(1−d)/0.0` — `+inf` inside, `−inf` outside, both clamping to the *correct* answer. The guard is load-bearing only at exactly `d == 1.0`, where it is `0.0/0.0`. ⚠ **IEEE infinity arithmetic can make a missing guard invisible.**
- **M3.1b, `dragging_an_edge_resizes_about_a_fixed_centre`**: dragged horizontal edges with `dy = 0` and vertical ones with `dx = 0`, so a cross-axis leak was a no-op it could not see. ⚠ **A zero component in a test's input is a blind spot; a real mouse drag never has one.** Every drag is diagonal now.
- **M3.1b, `an_existing_linear_sidecar_still_loads`**: asserted the *variant* and not the *payload*. Under `#[serde(untagged)]` serde reads `LinearGradient` from the whole object, and its container-level `#[serde(default)]` makes the unknown `"Linear"` key be **ignored** and every field default — so the variant still matched with `from`/`to` silently `[0,0]`. ⚠ **`#[serde(default)]` makes a struct deserialize from almost anything**, the same trap `edit_clipboard`'s tagged envelope exists for.

**The eighth is the one to read if you only read one, because the *harness* was at fault rather than the assertion.** The navigation rework's `a_left_drag_does_not_pan` — the regression guard for that entire change — passed vacuously because `drag_delta()` is per-frame and the harness moved the pointer on an earlier pass than the one it read. The assertion was right; it was fed zeros. ⚠ **When a test drives a UI framework, check that the harness produces the event on the frame you read.**

**M3.1c-A added three more, all found by the sabotage step and none by review**, and they name three *different* ways a test can be blind:
- **`frame_map_agrees_with_geometry_stage_on_a_straighten_angle`** — no **leverage**. A rotation sign error displaces a point in proportion to its distance from the centre; the probe sat at 0.34 of the half-width, where the flip moves it 1.34 px, inside a 1.5 px tolerance. ⚠ **A geometric test needs leverage, not merely a plausible input.**
- **`a_dab_is_round_on_a_wide_frame`** — the sabotage was a **no-op on that fixture**. On an 80×40 frame the long edge *is* the width, so scaling the radius by width changed nothing. A portrait case discriminates.
- **`overlapping_dabs_in_one_stroke_do_not_double_up`** — the assertion **band was too wide**. Accumulation gave 181, inside `> 0 && < 250`. Comparing against a single dab *exactly* is what discriminates.

**And two that asserted something FALSE about correct code** — a related but distinct fault worth separating: `a_soft_dab_ramps_from_the_centre_outward` demanded exactly 255 at a dab centre that falls on a pixel *boundary* (peak is 254), and `an_eight_bit_edge_has_no_visible_banding` asserted `<= 8` against a ramp whose own analytic slope is ~12. **When a new test fails, ask whether the assertion is right before touching the code.**

**⚠ PARTLY ADDRESSED 2026-08-26 — a fixture now drives the real `canvas::show`; see "Headless canvas tests". The finding below still holds for the PANEL, and for appearance anywhere.**

**⚠⚠ A DIFFERENT and larger finding, from M3.1c-B: the suite tests arithmetic well and interaction hardly at all.** Six bugs in that feature, **every one found by using the app and none by a test** — while the pure functions beside them (`FrameMap`, `inverse_point`, the rasterizer, decimation) carry ~30 tests and produced no defect at all. All six were canvas wiring: a crash from a mesh representation, a stale render, a cached screen rect, a bbox with no outside value. ⚠ **Headless `RawInput` tests of the drag lifecycle would have caught at least three.** The seam exists and works — `clicking_a_library_grid_thumbnail_opens_it`, `a_left_drag_does_not_pan`, `tool_drag_for` — it is simply not used for the canvas. **Treat canvas and panel work as untested territory.**

**⚠⚠ And a process finding that invalidates any sabotage result: a sabotage that fails to COMPILE looks exactly like one that passes.** Placing `#[serde(untagged)]` above the `#[derive]` instead of below made the edit an error; grepping for a test result found nothing, which reads as a clean pass. **Confirm the sabotage compiled before believing it.** Later tasks ran every sabotage through a helper that checks for compile errors first.

**A seventh, in a golden rather than a test:** M3.1b's exposure golden clipped at +1.0 EV — six of twenty samples pinned at 255, so it could not tell the ellipse's peak coverage from any larger value. That is the *identical* weakness 3.1a recorded for its linear exposure golden and did not fix. Now +0.55 EV, nothing clipped, 14 of 20 discriminating. ⚠ **Measure a generated golden, do not just look at it** — clipping is invisible to the eye and fatal to discriminating power.

**The habit that catches all of them: after writing a test, break the thing it names and watch it fail.** Every `⚠ watched failing` note in this file is one of those. When a sabotage step *doesn't* fail, that is a finding about the test, not a formality to wave through — one of this session's subagents refused to move on for exactly that reason and was right.

**M3.1c — the brush. Decisions already taken in brainstorming (2026-08-18), before the navigation rework preempted it:**

- **Storage: vector strokes in `Edits`, rasterized to a cached coverage buffer.** `Vec<Stroke>` (points, radius, hardness, erase) is small, exact, resolution-independent and remappable. The renderer rasterizes once per render into a frame-space coverage map. Rejected: a raster buffer *in* `Edits` (soft at 1:1, bloats every sidecar, makes "undo one stroke" impossible); and per-pixel evaluation over every stroke (O(strokes × points) per pixel).
- **⚠⚠ Anchoring: brush strokes live in PRE-geometry (source image) coordinates. Linear and radial keep frame coordinates.** This is the biggest finding of the brainstorm and it is a **latent bug the brush makes acute**, structurally identical to `invert` having no UI in 3.1b. `MaskStage` runs in the **tail**, after `GeometryStage`, so `u = (x0+x)/fw` is normalised against the *already-cropped, already-rotated* frame — **change the crop and every mask slides over the image content.** Invisible for a gradient, tolerable for a radial, ruinous for a stroke painted onto a subject. The asymmetry is principled, not a hack: **a gradient is compositional** ("darken the top" should stay at the top after a re-crop) **and a stroke is content-referred** (it must stay on the cat). Existing sidecars are untouched, which is the other reason not to convert all three.
- **⚠ The rasterizer, not `MaskStage`, applies the geometry transform.** Because coverage is rasterized into a *frame-space* buffer, `MaskStage` just samples it and never learns geometry exists. Rotation preserves circles and the crop does not stretch, so a dab of radius R source-pixels splats as a disc of radius R×scale frame-pixels — O(dabs × dab area), not O(pixels × dabs).
- **⚠ The cache key must NOT include the region**, exactly as `PrefixCache`'s does not — rasterize whole-frame, sample the sub-rect, so panning never invalidates.
- **⚠ Suggested seam, following the `Region` precedent:** `MaskStage` rasterizes internally when handed no pre-built coverage, so the CLI, export and all 35 goldens compile untouched; the engine passes a cached buffer when it has one. That is the pattern that gave the 1:1 region work "zero call-site churn".
- **Controls: size, hardness, erase.** Erase earns its place — overshooting is inevitable and undoing the whole stroke throws away the good part of the same drag.
- **⚠ Flow is deferred to a later roadmap milestone** (user's call, 2026-08-18). It complicates stroke-overlap handling in the rasterizer, which is the part worth keeping simple while 3.1c proves the architecture. **The roadmap file in the Obsidian vault still needs this note added.**
- **⚠ `Mask` and `MaskedAdjustment` lose `Copy`** — a stroke list cannot be `Copy`. Three sites depend on it today: `stages/mask.rs:35` (`masked: *masked`), and `develop/canvas.rs:309`/`330`. ⚠ `mask_tool::apply_drag(m: Mask, ..) -> Mask` should become `&mut Mask` in place rather than clone a stroke list on every drag frame.
- **⚠ Coverage map as `u8`, not `f32`** — a full-res frame map is 26 MB rather than 104 MB, and 256 levels across a soft edge is below the visibility threshold.
- **Still open:** whether `MaskStage`'s bounding-box optimisation (deferred at 3.1a and again at 3.1b) comes in here. Brush is the case that motivates it, but measure before assuming.

**After the navigation rework, resume from here.** The original framing follows.

**M3.1c — the brush.** The last of M3.1's three primitives, and the one that **closes the milestone** — M3.1's own "Done when" names dodge/burn with a brush. Only then does the parked button/layout work resume.

⚠ **Read M3.1b's "did the architecture hold" verdict first** (`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-18-radial-mask-design.md`). Short version: it held, and the one structural item was `mask_tool`'s `Handle` enum. **The brush will test that decision much harder**, because a stroke's "handles" are not points at all — there is no fixed set of five positions to hit-test. That is where the rejected nested-`Handle` option becomes worth revisiting, and where the flat enum may genuinely stop paying.

⚠ **A brush is not a fourth analytic shape, and this is the real difficulty.** `Mask::coverage(u, v, aspect)` is a pure function of position for both existing variants; a brush is *sampled data*, so it needs storage (a stroke list or a coverage buffer), a resolution policy, and an answer to what happens on a crop or an orientation change. Expect this one to need architecture, unlike 3.1b. It is also the first mask whose `MaskStage` cost argues for the bounding-box optimisation deferred twice now.

⚠ **Two compile errors will greet the variant** — `Mask::coverage`'s match and `paint_mask`'s — plus `Mask::label()` and `Mask::invert_mut()`. Note 3.1b predicted three and got one, because Tasks 2 and 3 prepared the seams *before* adding the variant; that sequencing is worth repeating.

The **visual identity** itself (actual colours, icons, symbols) is cheap to defer, because anything expressible in `egui::Style`/`Visuals` is one central change applied retroactively. What is expensive to defer is *structural* — whether buttons carry icons, whether sections have headers — since every feature added meanwhile multiplies the instances to convert.

**Virtual copies (M3.4) — several edit versions per file (2026-08-25).** Copies are Library rows, sessions, sync targets and exports. 858 → **888 tests**; 47 goldens untouched, which is the proof no pipeline code moved. **Snapshots are NOT included** — a separate slice, backlogged.

⚠⚠ **READ THE "Naming, renaming and the browser" SECTION BELOW BEFORE TRUSTING THIS ONE.** Three of its bullets were **overturned** by a week of using the feature — the "one number used everywhere" rule, "copy numbers are never reused", and what `_N` means in an export filename. They are marked ⛔ **SUPERSEDED** in place. Everything else here still holds.

- **⚠⚠ THE WHOLE FEATURE IS AN IDENTITY CHANGE.** `PathBuf` *was* a photo's identity in six places — the sidecar name, `BufferCache`, `HistoryStash`, `Session`, sync targets and export naming. `PhotoId { path, copy }` replaces it. Everything else here is small by comparison.
- **⚠⚠ `BufferCache` STAYS KEYED BY PATH, AND THAT IS WHAT DELIVERS "AT NO STORAGE COST".** Copies of one file share one decoded buffer (78–313 MB), so opening a copy of an already-decoded file is a cache *hit*. It required no work — only a decision not to change something, which is precisely the kind of decision that gets undone later. `copies_of_one_file_share_one_decoded_buffer` guards it, and ⚠ **the sabotage does not merely fail, it fails to COMPILE** — a stronger guard than a runtime assertion.
- **⚠⚠ THE MIGRATION'S DANGER NEEDS TWO THINGS, NOT ONE — the design said otherwise and was wrong.** The claim was that an untagged `{ Envelope, Legacy }` enum would read every existing bare-`Edits` sidecar as an empty copy list, destroying every edit with a green suite. **Measured: untagged alone is harmless** — `Sidecar::copies` is a *required* field, so a legacy object fails to match and correctly falls through; all 15 tests passed. It becomes data loss only when `#[serde(default)]` is **also** added to `Sidecar`, the obvious "forward compatibility" move. So there are **two independent guards**, both commented: explicit key dispatch in `load_all`, and the deliberate *absence* of `#[serde(default)]` on `Sidecar`.
- **⚠⚠ A PRODUCTION BUG THIS INTRODUCED AND CAUGHT: two readers of one format.** Once the sidecar became an envelope, `sidecar::load` still parsed files as a bare `Edits` and returned `None` for **every** file the new code writes. Its live caller was `reload_if_affected`, so **a synced photo would silently reset to neutral**. Found by two failing sync tests, not by reading. Fixed by removing the trap rather than patching the site: `load`/`save`/`persist` are now thin **copy-0 wrappers** over the copy-aware API, so one reader exists instead of two.
- **⚠⚠ THE STASH AND THE LOG HAD TO RE-KEY TOGETHER, and this file already said so.** From the per-photo-edit-history work: *"The storage was the easy half; the log was the feature. A per-path history alone does nothing, because `ActionKind::PhotoEdit` was anonymous."* Re-keying `HistoryStash` on `PhotoId` while `PhotoEdit` still carried a bare path would make Ctrl+Z on the original pop a *copy's* stack. One mechanism, one commit — and each half has its own sabotage-verified test.
- **⚠ The plan's own warning paid off: after the session work, every surviving `PhotoId::original` in `main.rs` was suspect. Six existed; FOUR were bugs** — including `stash`, which would have made two copies of one file share an undo stack. The two that remain are legitimate (single-file open, and a fallback when the pending id is gone).
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — ONE NUMBER USED EVERYWHERE.** The identity number *is* the displayed number *is* the export suffix. ⚠ **Deliberately not Lightroom's labelling**, which calls the original "Copy 1" — and the mockup this was chosen from followed Lightroom. A display number offset from the identity is two facts that can disagree, the failure `render_max_dim`, `fit_image_size` and `geometry_mode` were each fixed for. **⛔ Now: the number is still the identity, but a *named* copy shows and exports its own name instead. The rule survives in the form that matters — the name is an explicit, user-chosen override, not a second derived fact.**
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — Copy numbers are stable and never reused** (`max + 1`). Renumbering after a delete would silently change a copy's export filename and invalidate its history-stash key. **⛔ Still true of everything automatic; `Reindex` is the one explicit action allowed to renumber, and it drops the affected undo stacks for exactly the reason above.**
- ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — TWO NUMBERING SCHEMES IN EXPORT FILENAMES, with different separators on purpose.** `_N` is **identity** — which copy, stable forever. `-N` is **de-duplication** — the Nth file wanting this name in this folder, assigned at export time, and it already existed for Keep Both. They compose: copy 2 exported twice gives `a_2.jpg` then `a_2-1.jpg`. Merging them would make `a-2.jpg` ambiguous and would shift a copy's number whenever its plain name was taken. **⛔ Now: `_N` is only the *default* name of an unnamed copy. `-N` is unchanged, and it is what stops two copies sharing a name from overwriting each other — a second, stronger reason not to merge the two schemes.**
- **⚠ Sync's `prior` is per FILE and deduplicated, not per copy.** Two copies of one file are two read-modify-write cycles on one sidecar, so a second snapshot would capture the *first* target's change and undo would restore that. ⚠ And `SyncAction` had to keep its `targets` explicitly, because a per-file `prior` cannot say which *copies* were synced — redo would otherwise re-sync copy 0 of every file involved.
- **⚠ The name is envelope metadata, never an `Edits` field.** In `Edits` it would need an edit group and would **travel with a paste, a preset or a sync** — pasting a B&W recipe onto another photo would rename it — and "Reset adjustments" would wipe it. The cost is that `History<Edits>` cannot undo it, which is why renaming gets its own `ActionKind`. ⚠ Undo and redo call **one** function with the arguments swapped, so the two directions cannot drift. (The variant is now `CopyRename`; see the next section for why file renames needed a *second*, global one.)
- **⚠ The rename field edits a scratch draft**, like the export dialog's. Writing through would push one undo step per keystroke and commit a half-typed name on focus loss. Empty normalises to `None`; unchanged commits nothing, or Ctrl+Z would appear to do nothing.
- **⚠ `entries::build` is a pure function with the sidecar loader INJECTED**, so every expansion rule is tested without disk or a GUI — deliberately, because the Library is where this project's three sparse-index bugs happened. ⚠ **The original is always a row even when the sidecar omits it**: `persist_copy(.., 1, ..)` on an unedited photo writes copy 1 and no copy 0, and omitting it would make the photo vanish after copying it.
- **⚠ `rebuild_entries` rebuilds `thumbs` and the loader too**, because both are index-parallel and the indices have just shifted. `Library` keeps its `scan` so the re-expansion needs no second directory read and sees the same files.
- **Measured: reading every sidecar on folder open costs 4.6 ms for 830 files** (0.006 ms/file), against a 100 ms threshold that would have forced the read onto the loader thread. `cargo run --release -p app --example sidecar_scan <folder>` reproduces it.
- **⚠ ⌘' IS safe as a native accelerator, and knowing why matters**: the hazard is a **bare** key (stolen from every text field) or a text-editing chord (⌘Z/C/V/X/A). A plain apostrophe typed into the rename field is not the ⌘' chord. ⚠ `every_shortcut_maps_to_an_egui_key` caught the missing `'` → `Key::Quote` mapping, and the fix was to **complete the table, not weaken the test** — the rule already recorded for ⌘C/⌘V.
- **⚠ A test-isolation bug worth knowing: two tests reused the temp-dir tag `"absent"`, and `TempDir::new` does `remove_dir_all` — so in parallel they deleted each other's directory.** It surfaced as an *unrelated pre-existing test* failing during a sabotage run, which is the worst possible moment to be chasing a phantom. Fixed structurally (process id + monotonic counter), the same remedy `image-decode`'s `temp_path` needed after taking uniqueness from the clock.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **copies share a pixel-identical thumbnail** — Library thumbnails are the camera's embedded JPEG, so a black-and-white copy shows in colour, told apart only by badge and name; edit-accurate thumbnails are backlogged with this motivation attached; (2) **snapshots are not included**; (3) folder open now reads every sidecar, where it read none; (4) copy numbers are never reused, so a churned photo can reach `_47` with three copies; (5) two numbering schemes in export filenames; (6) **a copy is not a file** — nothing outside Rustroom knows it exists, and the sidecar is the only record; (7) single-file mode ("Open file…") lists no copies, since it has no Library.

**Library polish (2026-08-27) — persistent filters, a photo context menu, and rubber-band selection.** 1114 → **1124 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. Both changes came from using the finished M4.3b.

- **⚠⚠ A REVERSAL: THE FILTER NOW SURVIVES A SOURCE SWITCH, AND THE EARLIER REASONING WAS WRONG.** M4.3a-ii reset the search text and mark filters on every switch, argued as *"a stale search that empties a newly opened folder reads as breakage."* In use it is the opposite: **browsing replaces the source**, so filter-then-pick-a-date is the *only* way to say "five-star picks from the 9th", and the reset destroyed exactly the composition that browse-replaces-source otherwise denies. ⚠ **The `Filter: …` label is what makes persistence safe** — a filter hiding everything is visible and one click from fixed — and that label did not exist when the reset was chosen.
- **⚠ The filter moved to `RustroomApp`, it was not copied across in `open_scan`.** Copying works and was rejected: it leaves two places that can disagree about which filter is current, the shape this project has paid for three times (`render_max_dim`, `Session::geometry_mode`, the thumbnail loader's shadow copy). `Library::refilter` takes the filter **by reference**, which keeps `Library` ignorant of the app — that is what lets `library_of` build one in a test.
- **⚠ `open_scan` applies the filter explicitly rather than leaving it to `reload_marks`**, which early-returns without a catalog. The filter must hold across a switch whether or not one is open.
- **⚠ Session-only, deliberately.** The *format* half stays separately persisted in `Settings`, because a format preference is standing where a search string is about the question being asked now.
- **⚠⚠ A NEW `Capture` FIELD CANNOT BE BACKFILLED UNDER THE ROW-PRESENCE DESIGN — found while planning this, and it is the reason dimensions are read live.** `files_without_capture` queues files with **no capture row**, so adding `width`/`height` columns would leave all 832 already-scanned files NULL *forever*: the new fields would apply only to photos nobody owns yet. Fixing that needs a `capture_version` column and a re-read pass. **Reading one photo's dimensions when a dialog opens needs neither**, costs ~15 ms once, and can never be stale.
- **⚠⚠ A RAW AND ITS JPEG SIBLING LEGITIMATELY DIFFER IN SIZE, and the first version of that test asserted otherwise.** Measured: the RAW is **6246×4170**, the camera's JPEG **6240×4160** — libraw's active area includes border pixels the camera crops, and 6246×4170 is exactly what a full decode produces. ⚠ **This is where dimensions differ from camera and date**: those are facts about the *shot* and are normalized to agree exactly; dimensions are a property of the *file*. The test asserts the **orientation** agrees and the sizes are within 2%. Fourth "assertion false about correct code" this session.
- **⚠ Both readers apply orientation, by different routes.** libraw reports the *sensor's* dimensions, so `dimensions` swaps them for `sizes.flip` of 5 or 6 — CLAUDE.md records `sips` doing the same thing sending an investigation down a blind alley. `image_dimensions` likewise ignores EXIF, so the four transposing `Orientation` variants are **named rather than a numeric `5..=8` range**, which would silently mean something else if the crate reordered the enum.
- **⚠ The Information dialog reads its dimensions ONCE, into the modal's own state.** A dialog body runs sixty times a second, and a whole-file read there is the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in its most expensive costume yet. ⚠ Every absent value shows as `—`, never a blank row: a missing field is *information*, and two of the author's exported JPEGs carry no EXIF at all.
- **⚠ Shutter is shown as a fraction below one second.** The stored value is `0.0199999995529652`; showing that is technically honest and practically useless.
- **⚠ A secondary click does NOT fire `clicked()`**, so the context menu selects the photo explicitly — otherwise Edit and Rename would act on whatever was selected before rather than on what was right-clicked.
- **⚠ One `apply_cell_action`, shared by the grid and the filmstrip**, and Rename reuses `name_edit`'s draft rather than adding a second path. Two editable-name mechanisms drifting is the bug that made copy names invisible in the grid.
- **⚠ A commit went in with clippy failing again** — `field assignment outside of initializer`, in two tests. `cargo test` was green, which is not evidence about clippy. Amended. **Check clippy's exit code before every commit**; this is the second recorded instance.
- **⚠⚠ TWO RENAME-FIELD BUGS, BOTH FROM USING IT, AND THEY HAD DIFFERENT CAUSES.** (1) **Returning from Develop to the Library activated the rename field.** The Develop panel draws its name field on *every* frame, so `get_or_insert_with` leaves a draft behind for whatever photo was open — harmless there, since autofocus is deliberately off in Develop, but the Library grid draws the field for whichever row owns the draft **and autofocuses it**. Fixed by clearing `rename_draft` on a view switch, beside the status line, which is per view for the same reason. ⚠ Discarded rather than committed: renaming is explicit, and navigating away is not a way to confirm a half-typed name. (2) **Clicking empty space did not close the field** — it commits on `lost_focus()`, and clicking empty grid space moves focus *nowhere*, because nothing there is focusable. `field` now surrenders focus on a press outside its own rect, which makes `lost_focus()` fire on the next frame and reach the one commit path. ⚠ The pointer is read into a local **before** `r` is touched — the re-entrant context lock this very function already paid for once.
- **⚠⚠ RUBBER-BAND SELECTION, AND THE HIT-TEST ORDERING IS THE WHOLE TRICK.** A drag-sensing background is registered **before** the cells, because egui breaks a tie by registration order (`hit_test.rs`: *"In case of a tie, take the last one = the one on top"*). Registered after, it would swallow every click meant for a thumbnail. The scrollbar is drawn by the `ScrollArea` afterwards and is therefore on top, so dragging it still scrolls. Cells sense `click_and_drag` too, so a band can also start on a photo.
- **⚠ `banded` takes the drag's two CORNERS, not a rectangle — and that was a correction.** With `Rect::from_two_pos` at the call site, the "backwards drag" test built *both* rectangles itself and so exercised **egui's** normalisation rather than ours: it passed with ours deleted. Moving the normalisation into `banded` put the behaviour where the test can reach it. Another test naming a bug it could not detect, caught by the sabotage step.
- **⚠ Intersects, not contains.** A band must catch a photo it merely clips, or selecting a run would mean drawing the rectangle precisely around every thumbnail.
- **⚠ The additive case unions and re-selects rather than toggling.** A toggle would *deselect* a photo the band passed over twice, so sweeping back and forth would flicker the selection.
- **⚠ Only what is drawn can be banded**, because the grid is virtualised — which is also all a band can cover, since it cannot leave the viewport. There is no auto-scroll while dragging.
- **⚠ A click on empty space clears the selection, and only a PLAIN click does.** A ⌘- or Shift-click there is part of *building* a selection, not a request to throw it away — `apply_click`'s rule. ⚠ A click and a band cannot both fire from one gesture: egui reports `clicked()` only for a press-release that did not become a drag, so the two are mutually exclusive by construction rather than by a threshold.
- **⚠⚠ CLIPPY CAUGHT A FEATURE THAT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED DEAD.** `cell_rects` was flagged as never-mutated: the edit adding the rect collection had not landed, because the surrounding indentation differed from the pattern it was applied with. The band would have compiled, run, and **selected nothing** — and no test would have noticed, since the `banded` tests use a synthetic strip. Third commit-time clippy save this session; the earlier two were `field assignment outside of initializer` and an unused variable.
- **⚠ Deferred by the user's call: shift/⌘-click multi-select of dates**, folded into **M4.3c** because multi-select periods and smart collections both want *"a source described by a rule"*, and building it first risks building it twice.
- **⚠ Six fields are stored and still not filterable** — ISO (11 values), aperture (10), shutter (8), focal length (33), file size, and edited-state. ⚠ Focal length wants a **range slider** rather than checkboxes at 33 values; the rest are list-sized. Held back deliberately until the Information dialog has been used, since seeing the values per photo is what says whether filtering on them is worth it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) the filter is session-only, so it resets on restart while the format half does not; (2) **Information reads the file**, so opening it on a RAW costs ~15 ms; (3) no Delete, Export or Copy in the context menu — three actions, not an exhaustive menu; (4) dimensions are not stored, so they cannot be sorted or filtered on; (5) **no auto-scroll while banding**, so a selection cannot run past the viewport in one gesture; (6) a rename left half-typed is discarded by switching view, not committed.

**Filtering on capture metadata (M4.3b, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-27) — M4.3b IS COMPLETE.** Browse by capture date from the left panel; narrow by camera and lens from the Filter popup. 1096 → **1114 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ THE DESIGN WAS CHOSEN FROM THE REAL DATA PLAN 1 COLLECTED, AND IT INVERTED THE ROADMAP'S PRIORITY.** Measured on the author's catalog: **1 camera, 2 lenses, 2 months, 6 days**. So the **camera filter — the criterion the roadmap actually named — has zero discriminating power on this library**, and a *month* filter nearly none, since 738 of 830 photos fall in one month. **Date at day granularity is the one that does real work.** ⚠ A ten-year archive inverts that, which is why no single granularity could be chosen and the tree expands instead.
- **⚠⚠ DATES ARE BROWSED FROM THE PANEL, NOT FILTERED — and that is what makes them need NO DATE PICKER, which egui does not have.** The tree is a `GROUP BY` over what is in the catalog, so it shows the six days that exist rather than asking anyone to type a range. It is also the idiom the panel already uses four times (All Photos, Folders, Collections, Keywords: *a list with counts, click to browse*). ⚠ **The cost, stated: "in my Portfolio collection, show me July" is not expressible** — the limitation keywords already have, and exactly what **M4.3c** exists to fix.
- **⚠⚠ `strftime` OVER THE STORED VALUE IS THE PAYOFF OF PLAN 1'S WALL-CLOCK DECISION.** `capture_days` groups with `strftime('%Y'|'%m'|'%d', taken, 'unixepoch')` and gets back **exactly the date the camera wrote**, with no timezone arithmetic anywhere in the app. Had `taken` been a true instant, every query here would need a zone.
- **⚠ The catalog returns DAYS and the app aggregates upward** (`date_tree::group`), rather than the catalog building a tree. The useful granularity depends on the library, and a flat day list is the one shape that serves both ends of that range. ⚠ `group` relies on the rows being **ascending**, which `capture_days`' `ORDER BY` guarantees — the alternative is a `HashMap` and a re-sort for no gain.
- **⚠ `date_tree` matches on the YEAR, not just the month.** Grouping on the month alone folds July 2025 into July 2026; `the_same_month_number_in_two_years_does_not_merge` is the guard, and the sabotage fails it.
- **⚠⚠ A CAMERA/LENS SECTION IS SHOWN ONLY ABOVE ONE VALUE.** With a single camera it would be one checkbox that can only hide everything, which is not a filter — the same reasoning that hides the format chips below two formats. On this library that means **the Camera section does not appear at all**, which is the correct response to the data rather than a gap.
- **⚠ A photo with no camera recorded FAILS a non-empty camera filter** (`is_some_and`). "Show me the X-T4 shots" must not include a file whose camera is unknown — and with no filter set it is shown like anything else.
- **⚠ `cameras`/`lenses` are `BTreeSet<String>`, not bitmasks like the mark filters** — the values are open-ended strings discovered from the catalog, not a fixed small enum.
- **⚠ `Library.captures` is keyed by PATH where `Library.marks` is keyed by `PhotoId`, and both carry a comment saying so.** Capture is per *file* and every copy shares it; a rating is per copy. Read **once per rebuild** via `captures_in` — one query per folder, never one per photo, the rule `marks_in` already follows.
- **⚠⚠ `reload_captures` MUST ALSO RUN AFTER THE SCANNER DRAINS**, not only where `reload_marks` runs — otherwise a folder that has just been scanned stays unfilterable until something unrelated rebuilds. ⚠ Guarded on there being results, or it re-queries every folder sixty times a second: the `apply_view_demosaic` rule, now cited **eight** times in this file.
- **⚠ `id_salt` on every `CollapsingHeader` in the tree.** egui's auto-ids shift with what was drawn before them — the hazard the grid's `push_id` fixed — so two years with the same month number would otherwise collapse together.
- **⚠ `distinct_capture_column` interpolates its column name and is PRIVATE for exactly that reason**: SQLite cannot bind an identifier, so the safety rests entirely on both callers passing a literal.
- **⚠ One sabotage was inert and is recorded as such**: removing `WHERE taken IS NOT NULL` from `capture_days` changes nothing, because a NULL date makes `strftime` return NULL, the `i32` deserialization fails, and `filter_map(Result::ok)` drops the row. The clause is kept as defence in depth — relying on a swallowed error is not a thing to build on — but **no test can distinguish the two**, and inventing one that appeared to would be worse than saying so.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **a date cannot be combined with a collection or a keyword** — browse replaces, and composition is M4.3c; (2) **no arbitrary date range** — only a year, a month or a day from the tree; (3) the camera section is invisible on a one-camera library, by design; (4) **ISO, shutter, aperture and focal length are stored but not filterable** — they are `Capture` fields with no UI, and adding one is a `capture_section` call each once there is a reason; (5) a photo with no capture date is absent from the tree entirely, though it still appears in every other source.

**Capture metadata (M4.3b, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-27).** Date taken, camera, lens, ISO, shutter, aperture and focal length, read from every photo into the catalog by a background pass. 1078 → **1096 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on the real catalog:** schema v4 migrated with every row preserved, 832 files scanned, and **415 of 415 RAW+JPEG pairs agreeing on every field**.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED BEFORE DESIGNING, AND THE MEASUREMENT CHOSE THE ARCHITECTURE.** Reading a RAW's metadata is **~11–15 ms/file, of which libraw's parse is 0.2 ms** — 98% is pulling the ~29 MB file into memory, because `RawImage::open` takes a `&[u8]`. That is ~10–13 s for 830 files, which rules out doing it inline on folder open (today: instant, plus 4.6 ms of sidecar reads). `cargo run --release -p raw-decode --example bench_metadata <folder>`, kept.
- **⚠⚠ A PREFIX READ WAS TESTED AND REJECTED, AND *HOW* IT FAILED IS THE REASON.** At 64 KB, 256 KB and 1 MB libraw opens **nothing**; at 4 MB it opens **20 of 40**. A truncated read would work for part of a library and silently miss the rest — the worst failure shape, invisible until someone noticed a date filter quietly omitting photos.
- **⚠⚠ THREE FINDINGS FROM RUNNING BOTH READERS AGAINST THE SAME SHOT, none guessable from the documentation.** `cargo run --release -p app --example pair_check <raf> <jpg>`, kept, is the check the whole design rests on:
  1. **The readers disagreed about the camera's name** — libraw `"Fujifilm"`, EXIF `"FUJIFILM"`. A RAW+JPEG pair from one body would have appeared as **two cameras** in a filter. Same class as C4's profile matching, except here it is our own two readers disagreeing. **Fixed by normalizing at capture: `camera` is `"{MAKE} {model}"` with the make uppercased** — deterministic, no alias table, and what most bodies write in EXIF anyway.
  2. **`LensModel` comes back as the value plus 51 empty strings.** `display_value()` renders every one of them, so the naïve implementation stores `"XF23mmF2 R WR", "", "", …`. Read from `Field::value` and take the first non-empty. ⚠ `first_non_empty` is split out from `string_of` **so the padding is testable without building an EXIF container** — a test that cannot reach the finding is not a test of it.
  3. **RAW carries a timezone and JPEG carries none.** ⚠⚠ **Both store the local wall-clock as naive seconds**, via `.naive_local()` on the RAW side. Two reasons: the readers then agree *by construction* on a pair, where true UTC would make the RAW exact and the JPEG a guess about the machine's zone; and **wall-clock is what a photographer means by "date taken"** — the shots from the 17th means the 17th where they were standing. ⚠ Cost: photos from two timezones interleave by local clock rather than by true instant.
- **⚠⚠ THE PASS IS ITS OWN, NOT PIGGYBACKED ON THE THUMBNAIL LOADER — even though that already reads every file.** M4.3a-i made thumbnails **windowed**, so metadata attached to them would exist only for photos that had been scrolled past, and **you cannot filter by a date that was never read.** Accepted cost: a file is read twice on a first visit.
- **⚠⚠ `MetadataScanner::scan` is ADDITIVE where `ThumbLoader::want` REPLACES, and that difference is the point.** The thumbnail queue is "what is on screen now", so replacing it *is* the cancellation. This queue is "what still needs reading", and dropping it on a source switch would leave folders permanently half-scanned. It de-duplicates against everything ever queued, so re-opening a folder mid-pass costs nothing.
- **⚠⚠ AN ALL-`None` CAPTURE IS STILL STORED, and the real catalog shows why.** Two exported JPEGs in the author's folder carry no EXIF at all; skipping them would mean re-reading them on **every folder open forever**, 11–15 ms each, for nothing. `Some(Capture::default())` and `None` mean different things — *read and had nothing* versus *never read*. An unreadable file likewise yields an empty capture rather than being dropped.
- **⚠ Per FILE (`photo_capture` keyed on `(folder_id, file_name)`), like `photo_keywords` and unlike marks.** Capture metadata describes the *exposure*, which every copy of a file shares; a rating describes the image you made, which they do not. Third table to follow that split, and it carries the same comment because it looks like an oversight. ⚠ `files_without_capture` therefore needs **DISTINCT**: `photos` has a row per (file, copy), so without it a photo with three copies is read three times.
- **⚠ Schema v4 is a new gate (`if from < 4`), never an edit to 1–3** — third time that rule has met real user data. ⚠ **Both** migration-simulation tests needed `DROP TABLE photo_capture` added to their drop lists; the sabotage revealed there are two, not one, which reading alone had missed.
- **⚠ `Capture` lives in `core-image`, beside `Thumbnail` and for the same reason** — both decoder crates produce it. ⚠ `catalog` gains a dependency on `core-image` as a result; the alternatives were an eight-parameter `set_capture` (which trips `too_many_arguments`) or a duplicate row type.
- **⚠ libraw reports 0 for a value it does not have**, not an absence — `non_zero_u32`/`non_zero_f32` exist so "ISO 0" and "f/0" never reach a filter as real-looking options.
- **⚠ One new dependency, `kamadak-exif` 0.6** — pure Rust, **no C library, no `pkg-config` entry, no CI change**, the property that made pure-Rust `lensfun` acceptable. RAW needs nothing new: `rsraw` already exposes everything, `chrono` included.
- **⚠ Eleven sabotages, all caught**, including `Drop`-wakes-parked-workers — which a test counting *reads started* structurally cannot see, because a parked worker starts none. It asserts on `Arc::strong_count` of the shared queue, which **is** the live-worker count. Copied deliberately from `ThumbLoader`'s fifteenth-instance lesson rather than re-learned.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) ~10 s of *background* work the first time a folder is opened, then never again; (2) a file is read twice on a first visit; (3) **no re-read**, so a replaced file keeps its old metadata until the folder is forgotten; (4) wall-clock, not true instants; (5) camera and lens are free text with no alias table beyond the uppercased make; (6) **GPS is available from `rsraw` and deliberately not captured** — it needs a map to be worth anything; (7) PNG/TIFF get whatever EXIF they carry, usually none; (8) **nothing filters on any of this yet** — that is plan 2.

**The filter bar (M4.3a-ii, plan 2 of 2, 2026-08-27) — M4.3a IS COMPLETE.** Search by name, rating, flag and colour label, over whatever the Library is showing. 1052 → **1078 tests** (1067 as first shipped, plus eleven from two rounds of in-use fixes below); 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ ONE `LibraryFilter`, NOT FIVE MORE FIELDS ON `Library`.** It replaces `filter: FormatFilter` *and* `min_stars: u8` with a single value carrying format, text, stars, flag and label, and `refilter` became one call over the entries. ⚠ It also happens to be the **rule representation M4.3c needs** — a smart collection is a saved `LibraryFilter` — so that slice gets cheaper for no extra work here.
- **⚠ `FormatFilter` is CONTAINED, not absorbed.** Its *hidden-by-exception* rule is deliberate: it stores what is **hidden**, so a format appearing later is visible by default and needs no maintenance. Merging it would churn a tested thing for nothing. It gained one method, `is_active`, which landed with its caller per the bin-crate dead-code rule (tenth instance).
- **⚠⚠ TEXT MATCHES `entries::shown_name`, NEVER `file_name`.** That is the one rule the Library caption, the rename field and the default export name already share. Matching `file_name` would mean searching for what a copy is *called* and not finding it — precisely the bug that made copy names invisible in the grid, where the Library was the last consumer bypassing the shared rule. The sabotage fails two tests.
- **⚠⚠ STARS MATCH EXACTLY, VIA A MULTI-SELECT `RatingSet` — it shipped as a *minimum* and that was wrong, found in use within the hour.** A minimum cannot express *"only the one-star ones"*, which is a real culling question; and it made stars the odd one out, since flag and label already match exactly (`Some(Flag::Pick)` means picked, not "picked or better"). A set **subsumes** a minimum — 3-or-more is 3, 4 and 5 — at the cost of two extra clicks in a popup you are already in. Empty means "any", the convention `flag: None` and `label: None` already use, and `0` selects the unrated exactly as `Some(Flag::None)` selects the unflagged. ⚠ `RatingSet` is a `u8` bitmask whose `toggle` **ignores out-of-range values** rather than letting `1 << 9` wrap into another rating's bit.
- **⚠⚠ THE FILTER BUTTON IS ALWAYS PREFIXED `Filter: `, and it shipped without that.** Active, it read as `3★+` alone — so the button stopped naming itself and looked like a different control depending on what was set. Reported in use. `the_filter_button_reports_an_active_filter` asserts the prefix, and the sabotage fails with `the button stopped calling itself Filter: "3★"`.
- **⚠ The Filter button sits BESIDE the search field, not in the right-aligned cluster.** They are one idea — *narrow what I am looking at* — and splitting them across the row put half of it next to the sort order and the grid size, which are not. That also leaves the right cluster at its original five controls, which was the layout worry in the first place.
- **⚠⚠ ALL THREE MARK FILTERS ARE MULTI-SELECT SETS — `RatingSet`, `FlagSet`, `LabelSet` — and stars shipped as a minimum with flag and label as `Option`s before use corrected all three.** Empty means "any" in every one; the unset *value* (unrated / unflagged / unlabelled) is a real member, so "show me what I have not judged yet" is expressible and consistent. ⚠ Each is a `u8` bitmask whose `toggle` **ignores out-of-range values**, or `1 << 9` would wrap into another value's bit.
- **⚠⚠ ONE ORDERING ACROSS EVERY SECTION: the unset value first, then the values.** Flag read "Any, picked, rejected, unflagged" while colour read "Any, unlabelled, colours" — two orderings for one idea, reported in use. There is no "Any" row any more either: an **empty set is "any"**, which is the same convention in all three and one fewer thing to explain. ⚠ The flag ordering lives in **`FlagSet::ALL`**, beside the type, so the UI cannot re-invent it — the rule `EDIT_GROUP_NAMES` and `Tool::ALL` already follow.
- **⚠⚠ THE POPUP USES `PopupCloseBehavior::CloseOnClickOutside`, NOT egui's DEFAULT `CloseOnClick`.** Every row is a checkbox and the point is ticking several; a popup that shut on the first tick made setting two filters a four-click job of reopening it. Reached through `MenuButton::new(..).config(MenuConfig::default().close_behavior(..))` rather than `ui.menu_button`, which offers no config.
- **⚠⚠ THE SPEC'S LAYOUT WAS CORRECTED AFTER READING THE TOOLBAR, and the reason generalises.** The spec put the bar *"right of sort, left of the format chips"* — written before reading `shell.rs:143`, which is a single `Layout::right_to_left` cluster already carrying five controls **plus the status line**. A ~180 px text field there squeezes the status out, which is the *exact bug that cluster was created to fix*. So the **search field and the Filter popup both went on the LEFT** of the row, in the space the Develop toolbar occupies and which is empty in Library. ⚠ **The cost, stated: the star filter goes from one click to two**, and it is the most-used of the three.
- **⚠ The Filter button's label reports the active state** (`3★+ ⚑ unlabelled`, else `Filter`). A control that hides rows while looking idle is invisible state — the problem the crop aspect lock was fixed for — and `mark_filter_label` is a pure function so that property is testable without a window.
- **⚠ The glyphs were probed, not assumed** — `★ ⚑ ×`, the set M4.2a already shipped. ⚠ `every_filter_glyph_is_renderable` calls `theme::install_fonts` *and* runs a frame first: a bare `Context::default()` has not built its atlas and answers about egui's defaults, which production no longer uses.
- **⚠ No new keyboard work was needed, and that was checked rather than assumed.** The Library's bare culling keys (`0`–`9`, `p`, `x`, `u`), its arrow navigation and `pressed_command` are all already gated on `memory().focused()` — exactly the gate a text field needs. Escape to leave the field was added so the keyboard is recoverable without the mouse.
- **⚠⚠ THE FORMAT FILTER PERSISTS; THE REST OF THE FILTER DOES NOT — and that split is the point.** It shipped resetting on every source switch, which made *"I shoot RAW+JPEG and only want the RAWs"* something to re-set constantly. It lives in **`Settings`** now, beside `library_sort` and the cell size, so it survives a restart too. The search text and the mark sets still reset by **construction** — `open_scan` builds a fresh `Library` — because they are about the question you are asking right now, and a stale one that empties a newly opened folder reads as breakage. ⚠ `FormatFilter` gained `#[serde(transparent)]`, and `a_settings_file_from_before_the_format_filter_still_loads` guards the forgiving load, since `SettingsStore::load` is infallible and one unparseable field silently reverts *every* setting.
- **⚠ Both bodies guard on an actual change** before calling `refilter` — they run every frame, and refiltering hundreds of rows sixty times a second is the `apply_view_demosaic` mistake in another costume. That rule is now cited **six** times in this file.
- **⚠ A test that asserted something FALSE about correct code, the third this session.** `the_search_text_narrows_…` searched `"0001"` on twenty rows and expected one match; the right answer is **eleven** — `00001` plus `00010`–`00019`. Fixed in the test with a substring that occurs exactly once. **When a new test fails, ask whether the assertion is right before touching the code.**
- **⚠ Six sabotages, all caught, each by the tests that name it**: the four `matches` criteria individually, `file_name` for `shown_name`, and removing `refilter`'s `retain_visible` — that last one being the hazard where a filter hides a selected photo and a sync then writes to photos that are not on screen.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **the mark filters are two clicks now**, and "3 or more stars" is three ticks rather than one pick — the price of expressing *only* one star at all; (2) **no keyword in the bar** — panel only, since browsing a keyword *is* that operation once All Photos exists, and composing keyword-with-collection is what M4.3c is for; (3) **no saved searches** — M4.3c; (4) text is a plain case-insensitive substring: no globs, no regex, no field prefixes; (5) every mark filter is a set, so a *range* like "2 to 4 stars" is three ticks rather than a range control; (6) the search text and the mark sets reset on every source switch — only the format filter persists.

**"All Photos" and folder management (M4.3a-ii, plan 1 of 2, 2026-08-27).** The whole catalogued library is browsable as one view, and a folder can be removed from the catalog. 1044 → **1052 tests**; 47 goldens untouched.

- **⚠⚠ A TEST NAMED THE CASCADE IT COULD NOT SEE — the sixteenth instance, and found by the sabotage step within minutes.** `forgetting_a_folder_cascades_…` asserted on `all_photos`, `collection_members` and `keyword_members` — **all three JOIN through `folders`**, so with `PRAGMA foreign_keys = OFF` the orphaned rows simply drop out of every query and the test passed vacuously. The discriminating assertions are the **raw counts**: `photo_count()` is `SELECT count(*) FROM photos` with no join, and the counts inside `collections()` and `keywords()` likewise count their own tables. ⚠ The real damage of a missing cascade is invisible-but-permanent: orphans accumulate, and a folder re-added at the same path gets a **new id**, so the old orphans are unreachable forever.
- **⚠⚠ `mtime` IS FINALLY READ, after being stored and unused since M4.1.** CLAUDE.md recorded it as *"recorded and read by NOTHING… what a future re-link needs, and it costs nothing during a scan the caller is already doing."* A whole-library view is what it was for: `open_all_photos` builds a path→time lookup from the catalog and **does not walk the filesystem**, where `scan_of` used to `stat` every member — 10,000 stats on a view that otherwise touches no files.
- **⚠ `collections::scan_of` now takes `modified_at` injected, exactly as it already took `edited_at`.** One function, one rule — *the caller supplies the times*. Collections and keywords pass a `fs_modified` helper and are unchanged; All Photos passes the catalog lookup. The three existing tests kept their assertions verbatim, which is the evidence the refactor moved no behaviour.
- **⚠⚠ A FOLDER ROW RE-SCANS ON CLICK; IT DOES NOT BROWSE FROM THE CATALOG — and this reverses "one idiom for the whole panel".** That was the first instinct and it is wrong here: collections and keywords *span* folders and genuinely cannot scan, while a folder row names one real directory and can. Three consequences: there is no such thing as a stale panel-browsed folder; **refreshing needs no new button** (click the row again); and `Reindex` keeps working because the source is a `Folder`.
- **⚠ Deliberately NOT added: a lightweight "Refresh" beside "Reindex".** `Reindex` bundles two things — it re-scans *and* renumbers copies, which changes unnamed copies' export filenames and drops their undo stacks. Two buttons whose difference is "one of them also silently renumbers" is worse than one with a clear hover. Splitting `Reindex` into refresh-and-renumber is a real cleanup and belongs in its own change.
- **⚠ The re-scan had NO test until a sabotage said so.** Forcing `open_catalog_folder` down its catalog-fallback path left the entire suite green. `a_catalog_folder_row_rescans_the_directory` writes a file, loads, writes a second, loads again, and asserts the count went 1 → 2 — plus that the source stays a `Folder`, or `Reindex` would start refusing on it.
- **⚠ An unreadable directory falls back to the catalog's last-seen rows** rather than showing an empty folder. An unplugged drive must not look like the folder emptied itself — the rule `sync_folder` already follows in never deleting a photo row.
- **⚠⚠ FORGETTING DELETES CATALOG ROWS AND NEVER TOUCHES A FILE**, and that has its own test *with real files on disk* — the in-memory tests structurally cannot see it. This is the M4.1 split doing its job: *delete the catalog and you lose organisation, never work.* Its ratings, flags, labels and keyword assignments **do** go, which the dialog says plainly.
- **⚠ The forget dialog has no "don't ask again".** A deleted preset goes to a 20-entry trash and Ctrl+Z restores it; this has neither. Suppressing a guard on the *irrecoverable* action is exactly the distinction `the_two_delete_guards_are_independent` exists to protect.
- **⚠ Forgetting the folder on screen closes the Library; forgetting one while All Photos is open reopens it.** Two cases and both need handling — leaving either would show rows the catalog no longer has.
- **⚠ `open_scan` is now the ONE place a `Library` is constructed.** Three loaders had grown three copies of the same fifteen lines, and a fourth was the point at which they became one — a new field on `Library` had to be added in three places, which is how two of them drift. `open_folder` also split into the file dialog and `open_folder_at`, which is what lets a folder row reuse it.
- **⚠ The bin-crate dead-code trap fired exactly as predicted, for the ninth time**: `AllPhotos`, `open_all_photos` and `open_catalog_folder` are all dead until the panel calls them, so Tasks 2–4 landed as one commit.
- **⚠ Two sabotages were inert and are recorded as such**: deleting `photos` explicitly before the folder (the cascade would do it anyway) and — before the new test — forcing the catalog fallback. A sabotage that does not change behaviour is not evidence about the test.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **forgetting is destructive with no undo**, deliberately, and confirmed every time; (2) All Photos' mtimes are as fresh as each folder's last scan — clicking the folder row refreshes them; (3) All Photos lists photos whose files have vanished, the same *absence is not deletion* rule as collections; (4) no keyword filter in the bar — panel only; (5) **no filter bar yet at all** — that is plan 2; (6) no re-linking of moved files, unchanged from M4.1.

**Windowed browsing (M4.3a-i, 2026-08-27).** The Library grid and the filmstrip draw and load only what is on screen, bounded by a byte budget. 1019 → **1043 tests**; 47 goldens untouched, which is the proof nothing in `pipeline` moved.

- **⚠⚠ MEASURED, AND THE OLD COST WAS BEING PAID ON EVERY FOLDER OPEN — this is not speculative work for a library nobody has.** On the real 830-file folder (`cargo run --release -p app --example bench_library_open <folder>`, kept): **42.0 s and 552 MB** to fill the whole folder, against **1.5 s and 15 MB** for a first screenful. 28× and 37×. A 120-thumbnail window — a very large one — is 6.6 s and 79 MB. The steady state is capped by `THUMB_BUDGET_BYTES` at 256 MB however large the folder, so the ~7 GB a 10k source would have cost never happens.
- **⚠⚠ THE 10k "DONE WHEN" OF M4.3 LANDS HERE, NOT ON SMART COLLECTIONS, and that reframed the milestone.** "All Photos" is the first `LibrarySource` that can be large; `library.rs` drew *every* entry each frame, `Library.thumbs` held a texture per entry forever, and `ThumbLoader` ground a fixed list in ascending order with no notion of the viewport. Sliced out on its own so a grid bug could never be confused with a search bug — the M3.1a precedent.
- **⚠⚠ `ThumbCache` IS KEYED BY `PathBuf`, NOT BY AN `entries` INDEX, and three things fall out of one decision.** **(1)** Copies of one file share one texture — `entries` repeats a path once per copy, so a file with three copies held three pixel-identical 0.7 MB textures. **(2)** `rebuild_entries` invalidates nothing; it used to *have* to carry the textures across by hand, and getting that wrong greyed out the whole folder. **(3)** Indices never leave the UI, which removes the sparse-versus-dense bug family at the boundary instead of guarding against it downstream. `thumbnail_for` also collapsed from a position scan to a lookup.
- **⚠⚠ THE BUDGET WAS 256 MB AND THAT WAS TOO SMALL — found by using it, within an hour, and it is now 512 MB.** A **415-file folder measures 276 MB**, 20 MB over, so scrolling to the end and back evicted the top ~30 thumbnails and they visibly reloaded. Reported as *"the first like 20 thumbnails are greyed out"* — which is diagnostic, because generic scroll latency would grey whatever you land on, not specifically the *first* ones after a round trip. ⚠ The boundary was landing on ordinary folders; at 512 MB it holds **749** thumbnails, so any folder up to ~700 photos is browsed end-to-end and back with no eviction at all. ⚠ 512 also matches this project's own sizing precedent — `MID_BUDGET_BYTES` is 512 MB, and a *single* full-resolution prefix checkpoint is ~313 MB, larger than this whole cache was.
- **⚠ The sizing is a COMPILE-TIME assertion, not a comment** — `const _: () = assert!(THUMB_BUDGET_BYTES / TYPICAL_THUMB_BYTES >= 700)`, so lowering the budget back is a build error rather than a silent return of the eviction. Verified by doing exactly that and watching `E0080` fire. Same rule `MID_BUDGET_BYTES` already follows, and module-level rather than associated for the reason below.
- **⚠ The Library's scrollbar is pinned to the window edge (`auto_shrink([false, false])`).** It had tracked the *rightmost thumbnail*, moving whenever the column count or cell size changed, because egui's default puts the leftover width **outside** the scroll area. ⚠ **Pre-existing, not a regression from virtualisation** — the old `ScrollArea::vertical().show(..)` had the same default.
- **⚠⚠ THE WINDOW IS PINNED, NOT MERELY PREFERRED.** Eviction may never touch a path on screen, however far the budget falls, so the cache legitimately exceeds its budget under a very large window. Without it a big window silently blanks the cells being drawn, and the failure looks like a slow loader rather than a bug. Sabotage-verified.
- **⚠ `ThumbCache::get` takes `&self`; recency is set by `retain_window`.** What makes a thumbnail recent is *being on screen*, which is exactly what `retain_window` is told — and a `&mut self` accessor inside the draw loop would fight the shared borrows of `entries` and `visible` the loop already holds.
- **⚠ Generic over its value (`ThumbCache<T>`) so it can live in the lib**, which `lib.rs` says must not depend on egui. The bin instantiates `ThumbCache<egui::TextureHandle>`; the tests use `String`.
- **⚠ Replacing the want-list IS the cancellation.** `loader.want(&paths)` discards whatever was wanted before, so a scrolled-past request is simply never popped — "abandon, don't abort", the trade `DecodeWorker` and `batch_export` already make. An **in-flight set** stops a path being fetched twice, which matters because consecutive want-lists overlap almost entirely as the window moves.
- **⚠ The UI owns "what is loaded", not the loader.** The UI owns the cache, so it is the only thing that knows; a loader keeping a shadow copy would be two facts that can disagree — the failure `render_max_dim` and `Session::geometry_mode` were each fixed by deriving rather than storing alongside.
- **⚠⚠ WORKERS NOW PARK, SO `Drop` IS LOAD-BEARING — AND THE OBVIOUS TEST COULD NOT SEE IT (the fifteenth instance).** The old loader stopped because `tx.send` failed once `rx` was dropped; a worker asleep on the condvar never reaches `send`. `a_dropped_loader_stops_its_workers` counts *loads started*, and a parked worker starts none either way — so deleting the entire `Drop` impl left every test green while leaking up to six threads per folder open. `dropping_the_loader_wakes_parked_workers` asserts on `Arc::strong_count` of the shared queue, which **is** the live-worker count rather than a proxy for it, and it fails under that sabotage.
- **⚠⚠ VIRTUALISATION SILENTLY BREAKS THE RENAME FIELD, AND THIS HAZARD DID NOT EXIST BEFORE.** `name_edit::field` adds a `TextEdit` with no `id_salt`, so its id comes from egui's auto-id counter. `show_rows` calls `skip_ahead_auto_ids(min_row)` — it skips `min_row` ids while each row holds `cols` widgets, so the counter does **not** line up. Scroll one row with a rename open and every id shifts: the `TextEdit` loses its state, and worse, the *stale* id keeps focus so `autofocus`'s `focused().is_none()` guard never fires and the box goes dead. Fixed with `ui.push_id(&PhotoId)` per cell in both views. ⚠ **No test covers it** — found by reading egui's source while verifying the API, not by running anything, and confirmed only by hand.
- **⚠ `scroll_to_me` → `thumb_window::centre_offset`, and that is an improvement rather than a workaround.** egui computes a `scroll_to_me` target offset **once** and never recomputes it (`scroll_area.rs`), which is the M2.2 drift bug: the centred photo slid left as thumbnails ahead of it loaded and reflowed the content. Arithmetic placement has no such failure mode — and it is the only option once an off-window cell is never drawn.
- **⚠ The row-break bug is now structurally impossible**, so its test was *replaced*, not deleted. `cells_in_row(row, cols, n)` computes positions from the row directly; `rows_consume_the_visible_positions_densely_and_in_order` pins that the rows cover every position exactly once, in order, with only the last partial. `egui::Grid` is gone from the Library.
- **⚠ One window at a time**, which holds only because Library and Develop are never on screen together. If that changes they must contribute to a *union* rather than overwriting each other.
- **⚠⚠ TWO SABOTAGES ARE INERT, AND THAT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** Calling `want` unconditionally instead of on change, and `MARGIN_ROWS = 0`, both leave the whole suite green — because one is a per-frame cost and the other is latency, neither is correctness. Inventing a test that appeared to cover them would be worse than saying so. The five substantive sabotages (the pin, byte accounting on replace, LRU direction, the in-flight set, list replacement, the `Drop` flag, the window) were each caught by exactly one test.
- **⚠⚠ A HARNESS BUG, CAUGHT IMMEDIATELY, AND IT IS THE THIRD VARIANT OF ONE ALREADY IN THIS FILE.** My first sabotage runner grepped for `^error:` to detect a failed compile — but cargo prints `error: test failed, to rerun pass ...` for an ordinary *test* failure, so all three sabotages were reported as "DID NOT COMPILE" when in fact they had compiled and caught the bug. **The robust check is whether a `test result:` line appears at all.** Same root as the two variants already recorded: trusting the harness's output shape instead of checking it produces what you grep for.
- **⚠ A test that asserted something FALSE about correct code.** `a_replaced_want_list_abandons_what_is_no_longer_wanted` waited with `collect(&loader, 1)` — which returns on the *first* result to arrive, and that is one of the already-in-flight abandoned paths, never the newly wanted one. It waits for *that path specifically* now. Fixed in the test, not the code.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **fast scrolling shows grey cells briefly** — inherent to loading on demand; the margin softens it and does not remove it, and it is the caveat most likely to be reported as a bug; (2) one window at a time, as above; (3) the budget is a constant, not a setting — 256 MB from screen arithmetic, and it is the one number to turn down if memory proves tight; (4) an evicted thumbnail is re-read from disk, since there is still no on-disk thumbnail cache; (5) **nothing here is visible as a feature** except that folders become usable ~28× sooner.

**Keywords (M4.2b, 2026-08-26) — M4.2 IS COMPLETE.** Hierarchical keywords, assigned per file, browsable across folders. 1009 → **1019 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **Verified on the real catalog**: schema v3, a live `Europe > France > Paris` tree, and every folder, photo and collection membership intact.

- **⚠⚠ KEYWORDS ARE PER *FILE* WHILE MARKS ARE PER *COPY*, AND THAT IS NOT AN INCONSISTENCY.** A rating describes *the image you made* — a B&W copy can be better than the colour one. A keyword describes *what is in the picture*, which every copy of a file shares. So `photo_keywords` is keyed on `(folder_id, file_name)` while marks hang off `photos.id`. ⚠ **Both tables carry a comment saying so**, because it looks like an oversight.
- **⚠⚠ THAT KEYING MAKES DRIFT UNREPRESENTABLE.** Per-copy storage would need *two* rules — "assigning applies to every copy" **and** "a new copy inherits its source's keywords" — which can disagree. The failing case is concrete: tag copy 0 "Paris", browse a collection holding copy 1, search Paris, and copy 1 does not match. `a_copy_created_after_tagging_still_matches` pins it, and needs no inheritance code at all.
- **⚠⚠ `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX ... ON keywords(COALESCE(parent_id, 0), name)` IS LOAD-BEARING.** A plain `UNIQUE(parent_id, name)` allows **two root-level "Paris" keywords**, because **SQLite treats NULLs as distinct in a unique constraint** — silent duplicates that look identical in the tree with no way to tell them apart. Sabotage confirmed the index is the only thing preventing it. ⚠ And `the_same_name_under_different_parents_is_allowed` is the discriminating other half: "Paris" under both France and Texas is legitimate.
- **⚠⚠ IMPLIED PARENTS ARE DERIVED BY A RECURSIVE CTE, NEVER STORED.** Searching `Europe` finds photos tagged `Paris`. Storing the implication would mean rewriting every affected row whenever a keyword is re-parented — two facts that can disagree, the failure `render_max_dim` and `Session::geometry_mode` were each fixed for by deriving.
- **⚠ Hierarchy was chosen over flat because FLAT → HIERARCHICAL IS NOT AN AUTOMATABLE MIGRATION.** Nothing can infer that `Paris`, `Lyon` and `France` form a tree; the user would rebuild their taxonomy by hand. ⚠ "Flat now, unused `parent_id` in the schema" was rejected too — that hedge is justified for `size`/`mtime`, where a *scan* captures data that would otherwise be lost forever, but here it is speculative schema that does not spare the user the re-parenting, which is the actual cost.
- **⚠⚠ `rename_photo` NOW MOVES THREE THINGS** — the `photos` rows, and the `photo_keywords` rows; collection membership rides on the row id and needs nothing. Third instance of that integration. A rename that forgot would silently strip a file's keywords.
- **⚠⚠ EVERY NEW MIGRATION MAKES THE OLDER DOWNGRADE-SIMULATION TESTS INCOMPLETE.** `a_version_one_catalog_upgrades_and_keeps_its_rows` fakes a v1 catalog by *undoing* what later versions added — and it did not know about v3's tables, so `migrate` re-ran a step whose objects still existed and failed with "table already exists". **The failure is loud, but only because that test exists.** Add the new objects to its drop list whenever a version lands.
- **⚠ Deleting a keyword cascades to its children**, and their assignments. Destructive and deliberate: re-parenting orphans to the root would silently flatten a taxonomy you built, which is worse than an obvious deletion.
- **⚠ One panel, two sections — not two panels.** Collections and Keywords share one idiom: a list with counts, `+` to apply to the selection, click to browse. Two files would invite two idioms for one concept. Browsing a keyword reuses `collections::scan_of` and `LibrarySource` gained a `Keyword` variant, so the grid, filmstrip, format filter, sort orders and star filter all work unchanged.
- **⚠ `assign_keyword_to_selection` de-duplicates by path** — two copies of one file are one file. Assigning twice is a no-op anyway, but the status count would otherwise lie about how much was tagged.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **deleting a parent deletes its children**; (2) **no drag-and-drop** — a parent is chosen when the keyword is created; (3) **no renaming or re-parenting** once created, though the schema supports it; (4) **per file**, so one copy cannot be tagged differently — by design; (5) **catalog-only**, lost with the catalog, never travels with the photos; (6) **no keyword suggestions** — needs usage data and a heuristic, and it is polish on a feature that did not exist; (7) `unassign_keyword` exists and is tested but **has no UI** — the panel gains a remove control when re-parenting does.

**Ratings, flags and colour labels (M4.2a, 2026-08-26) — culling from the keyboard.** Three marks per virtual copy, badges in the grid and filmstrip, and a star filter. 993 → **1009 tests**; 47 goldens untouched. **The v1→v2 migration was verified against the real catalog**: 2 folders, 860 photos and 8 collection memberships all survived.

- **⚠⚠ M4.2 WAS THREE SUBSYSTEMS AND WAS DECOMPOSED BEFORE DESIGNING.** The roadmap bullet reads *"EXIF/IPTC display + edit; star ratings, flags, color labels; hierarchical keywords"*. Ratings shipped first (smallest, highest daily value — you cannot cull without them); **keywords are their own slice**; and **EXIF/IPTC editing went to `docs/BACKLOG.md` as a design conflict, not effort** — *writing* metadata means either modifying the original (which violates the thing Rustroom is built on) or a second XMP sidecar alongside the JSON one, reopening the interop question `02-architecture-and-tech` parked.
- **⚠⚠ THE V2 MIGRATION IS THE FIRST TO MEET REAL USER DATA.** A **new gate** (`if from < 2`), never an edit to version 1 — a migration that has already run on someone's machine cannot be changed. `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` means existing rows acquire "unmarked" with no backfill, and `0` is the neutral value for all three marks, so "no mark" needs no special case anywhere. `a_version_one_catalog_upgrades_and_keeps_its_rows` builds a v1 catalog *with a collection in it* and asserts the membership survives.
- **⚠⚠ `1`–`4` ALREADY SELECT RAIL TOOLS — exactly where Lightroom puts stars.** Resolved **by view**: digits cull in Library, select tools in Develop. The rail exists only in Develop and culling happens in the grid, and the two views already diverge on keyboard handling. Rejected: different keys for stars (nothing else is muscle memory — every photo tool uses digits, so it trades a real collision for an unlearnable binding) and moving the rail (breaks a shipped binding for an unshipped feature).
- **⚠⚠ THE CULLING KEYS ARE BARE, SO THEY SIT BEHIND THE EXISTING `memory().focused()` GATE.** The Library now has an editable filename field *and* a new-collection field; a `5` typed into either must not rate a photo. The **same** gate the grid's arrow navigation uses, not a second one. Also `!i.modifiers.any()`, so ⌘X stays cut and ⌘A stays select-all.
- **Marks are per COPY**, on the row `photos` already keys on — a rating describes *the image you made*, which is why copies exist. ⚠ **Accepted incoherence: rejecting a frame means rejecting each of its copies.** Splitting the rules (stars per copy, reject per file) was considered and rejected — two rules to explain, and a grid showing a rejected badge on rows whose stars differ. Lightroom is per-virtual-copy for all three.
- **⚠⚠ THE GLYPH GUARD EARNED ITS PLACE IMMEDIATELY: `✓` AND `✕` ARE IN NO BUNDLED FONT** and would have shipped as tofu boxes. Probing found the usable set is `★ ☆ × ■ ⚑ ⚐ ! ✚ ⊗ ▓`. Pick is `⚑` and reject `×` — and the flag is the *better* symbol anyway, since Lightroom's "pick" literally is a flag. Same finding the rail's four glyphs produced; **probe before choosing, never after**.
- **⚠⚠ A SABOTAGE EXPOSED A TEST THAT COULD NOT SEE THE LAYER IT NAMED — the thirteenth instance.** `an_out_of_range_rating_is_clamped_rather_than_stored` read the value back through `marks()`, which clamps on the **read** path too — so it passed with the *write* clamp deleted.
