- **⚠⚠ THE BANDING WARNING FIRES FOR ProPhoto ALONE, and the narrowing is the design.** The obvious rule is "any wide space + any 8-bit format" — but **8-bit Display P3 is what every iPhone ships by default** and **8-bit Adobe RGB has been the standard print-delivery format for twenty years**. A warning that fires on ordinary practice is a false alarm, and false alarms train people to dismiss the one that matters. `warns_in_8_bit()` carries that reasoning in its doc comment so nobody "completes" the table. **Warn, never block** — a blocked combination is the protective disable this project already shipped and took back out.
- **⚠⚠ A TEST THAT NAMED A BUG IT COULD NOT DETECT, caught by the sabotage step — the twelfth instance.** `the_prophoto_toe_meets_its_power_segment` probed either side of the **specified** threshold (1/512) while the sabotage moved the code's threshold to 0.01; both probes then landed inside the toe branch, where the bug is inert, and the real discontinuity sat at 0.01 untested. **A test that names the threshold cannot find a threshold that moved.** It now *sweeps* [0, 0.05] for a jump anywhere, and catches both a moved threshold and a wrong toe slope.
- **⚠ A test that asserted something FALSE about correct code**, caught on first run: `every_curve_clamps_to_the_encodable_range` demanded `encode(2.0) == 1.0`, but `1.055f32 - 0.055f32` is `0.99999994`. The arithmetic was fine and the clamp was working. Fixed the assertion, not the code — the lower bound stays exact, the upper one is a tolerance, and the comment says why that asymmetry is not an inconsistency.
- **⚠ The field-level `#[serde(default)]` on `ExportOptions.space` was REDUNDANT and was removed.** The struct already carries a **container-level** one, so a missing field falls back to the struct's `Default` regardless — verified by removing the attribute and watching the test still pass. What that test actually discriminates is the **value**: the default must be sRGB, since a wide space arriving by default would quietly change what every existing user's next export contains. Sabotaged with `Default` giving ProPhoto and watched to fail.
- **⚠⚠ Found by inspecting a real export, not by a test: every constructed profile reported as "RGB built-in".** lcms2's `new_rgb` writes no description tag, so `sips`, Preview and Photoshop showed all three as the same anonymous profile — indistinguishable to anyone checking a file by hand, and unverifiable without running our own code. Fixed by writing `ProfileDescriptionTag`; exports now read **"Display P3"**, **"Adobe RGB (1998) compatible"**, **"ProPhoto RGB compatible"**. `every_profile_names_itself` guards it. **The bytes always differed — this is about the file being legible.**
- **⚠ The preview stays sRGB, by construction rather than by discipline.** Only two call sites take a space (`rustroom/src/main.rs` and `batch_export::export_one`); the render engine, both split builders and every golden delegate with `Srgb`. `build_pipeline` keeps its signature and `build_pipeline_to` is additive — the `Region` precedent again, and again zero call-site churn.
- **⚠ `OutputTransformSrgb` → `OutputTransform`, and its stage name changed with it** (`output_transform_srgb` → `output_transform`), which six order-asserting tests named. The rename is right: the stage is no longer sRGB-specific.
- **⚠ Export presets carry the space for free**, because `ExportPreset` embeds `ExportOptions` whole rather than listing its fields.
- **Verified on a real X-T4 `.RAF`**: all four spaces write 16-bit TIFFs, each with its own profile name, each byte-different from the others; `--space prophoto` into a JPEG prints the banding warning and still writes; `--space rec2020` fails in milliseconds, before the decode, listing the valid tokens.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no gamut mapping** — out-of-gamut colour clips per channel rather than being perceptually compressed; rendering intents are a separate and much larger feature; (2) no 16-bit JPEG, so ProPhoto is only really usable via TIFF/PNG 16; (3) **the preview still cannot show P3** — piece **D**; (4) `rustroom decode`'s debug dump still asks libraw for sRGB; (5) the profiles say "compatible" because they are built from the same primaries and curve, not Adobe's or Apple's redistributed files.

**Colour management, plan 2 of 2: the working space is linear ProPhoto (2026-08-24).** libraw decodes with `output_color = 4`, the output transform converts ProPhoto→sRGB before encoding, and the primary-dependent stages take their luma from one `color::luminance()`. 712 → **714 tests**; **21 of 38 goldens moved**.

- **⚠⚠ The 17 goldens that did NOT move are the evidence the change was scoped.** All 38 moving at once would have destroyed this project's usual check; instead the unchanged set is exactly the primary-*independent* cases — `neutral`, `crop_center`, `straighten_5deg`, `orient_*`, `exposure_plus1`, `vignette_*`, `texture_*`, and the exposure-only masks. Every mover is a stage that reads primaries. That correspondence is stronger evidence than a count.
- **⚠⚠ `golden_neutral` passed WITHOUT regeneration**, which is the gate the whole plan hangs on: sRGB → ProPhoto → sRGB is a round trip through the real pipeline. ⚠ **If it ever fails, do not regenerate** — the error would be baked into all 38 references where nothing could detect it again.
- **⚠ The golden charts are authored in sRGB and converted at construction** (`set_srgb`). Their literals mean something — `[1.0, 0.0, 0.0]` is written to say "pure red", and read as ProPhoto it silently becomes a far more saturated colour than any camera produces.
- **⚠⚠ The same trap bit three test fixtures, and they are worth knowing as a family.** `hsl`'s `RED_ISH`/`BLUE_ISH`, `noise_reduction`'s `noisy_patch`, and `hsl`'s canonical `#FF0000…` colours were all raw values suddenly read as ProPhoto. Symptoms were *plausible*, not obviously broken: a hue rotation reading 22° instead of 30° (the chroma fade, because the colour's Oklab chroma moved), and chroma noise dropping only 0.90× instead of 0.3×. **Neither was a regression** — both were fixtures needing the same sRGB-authoring fix as the charts.
- **⚠ `BAND_HUES` needed NO change, and that is a result rather than luck.** Oklab hue is absolute, so the hue of sRGB red is the hue of sRGB red however it is expressed. The test now converts its canonical colours in; if that table ever has to move, the matrices are wrong, not the table.
- **⚠⚠ `image-decode` is the one this could most easily have shipped broken.** It hands the pipeline linear **sRGB**, which after the switch would be read as ProPhoto — every JPEG rendering heavily oversaturated. The spec never mentioned it; nothing in that crate looks at colour, so no test there would have caught it.
- **⚠ One commit, deliberately.** No ordering leaves the suite green partway: the instant the output transform converts, every golden disagrees until the charts, the stages *and* the decode agree. Granular commits are worth a lot, but not more than "every commit builds and passes".
- **⚠⚠ Measured on a real X-T4 `.RAF`, and the honest result has two halves.** On a **neutral render** the change is nearly invisible — max channel diff 11/255, mean 0.16, 0.0% of pixels differing by more than 4, identical mean saturation. That is correct, not disappointing: the subject holds no colour near sRGB's boundary, so nothing was being clipped. **Under a saturation push (+80/+40) the two diverge** — max 37, mean 1.47, 3.3% of pixels differing, and mean saturation **higher after** (0.3531 vs 0.3485). **The gamut win appears when you edit, not when you decode**, which is exactly the point: the old pipeline had already destroyed the headroom before any slider was touched.
- **⚠ A methodology note: the first before/after comparison was invalid and said so.** `git stash` had nothing to stash because the work was already committed, so both renders came from the same code and were byte-identical. The redo used a `git worktree` at `HEAD~1`. **A comparison that shows no difference should prompt "did I actually compare two things?" before "the change does nothing".**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) export is still sRGB-only — the output transform is now the seam where P3 and Adobe RGB drop in (piece **B**); (2) camera input profiles are still libraw's fixed matrix (piece **C**); (3) the preview is still sent to the display untagged, so a wide-gamut monitor still oversaturates it (piece **D**); (4) `rustroom decode`'s dump deliberately still asks libraw for sRGB.

**Colour management, plan 1 of 2: the `color` crate's wide-space groundwork (2026-08-24).** ProPhoto↔sRGB and ProPhoto↔Oklab matrices plus a single `lightness()`. 702 → **712 tests**; **every golden byte-identical**, which is the proof this changed no behaviour — nothing calls the new code yet.

- **⚠⚠ A WRONG FINDING, CAUGHT AND CORRECTED THE SAME DAY — read this one.** The design was specced on the claim that ProPhoto's luminance row (`R 0.2880, G 0.7119, B 0.0001`) "collapses blue", so working-space luma was unusable and Oklab L had to replace it. **That was wrong.** Luminance is a *physical* quantity: taking a colour into ProPhoto and measuring Y there returns what it always was — sRGB blue reads **0.0722** via Rec.709 and **0.0606** via ProPhoto's row; green 0.7152 and 0.7169. The `0.0001` applies to ProPhoto's own blue **primary**, a far more saturated blue that genuinely carries almost no luminance. **Generalising from the primary to all real colours was the error, and it was never checked against an actual colour before being written into a spec, a plan, and shipped code.**
- **⚠ What the correction bought.** `color::luminance()` returns ProPhoto's Y, and every constant tuned against linear luma — `local_contrast`'s `MID_GRAY = 0.18`, `sharpening`'s `LUMA_EPS`/`GAIN_MAX`/`HALO_KNEE`, `dehaze`'s airlight percentile — **carries over unchanged**. Sharpening, clarity and dehaze keep their character. A whole re-tuning pass, and a cube-root change to three tools, evaporated.
- **⚠ `luminance_matches_rec709` is the guard, and its absence is what let the error stand.** It checks the value against the Rec.709 result for *real* colours. Sabotaged by substituting sRGB's weights: the neutral test still passes (the weights sum to 1) and only the cross-space check fails — which is exactly the discrimination that was missing.
- **⚠ `prophoto_to_oklab` / `oklab_to_prophoto` are unaffected and still needed** — `hsl`, `noise_reduction`, `sharpening` and `local_contrast` use Oklab for *chroma* work, which was never the thing in question.
- **⚠ Colour management was never "absent".** `output_color = 1` means libraw already applies its camera matrix and hands us linear light in **sRGB primaries**. There *is* an input transform; the problem is that the space it lands in clips saturated colour before any slider is touched.
- **⚠ A fresh derivation of sRGB→LMS does NOT reproduce Ottosson's constants**: `0.4121764592` against his `0.4122214708`, ~4.5e-5 apart, because he rounded his primaries differently. **Do not "fix" `linear_srgb_to_oklab`** — its digits are transcribed from the reference. The cross-check test therefore uses **2e-3**, and tightening it produces a failure that looks like a broken matrix and is not one.
- **⚠ `an_srgb_colour_reaches_the_same_oklab` is the load-bearing test**: sRGB→ProPhoto→Oklab must agree with sRGB→Oklab directly, which validates the *entire* matrix chain against a completely independent path. Transposing two LMS rows leaves `grey_has_no_chroma_in_prophoto` **passing** — a row swap preserves neutrals — so greys alone would never catch it.
- **⚠ `white_maps_to_white` exists as a separate test because an un-adapted matrix pair is perfectly self-consistent.** Drop the Bradford D50→D65 adaptation from *both* matrices and the round trip still passes while every neutral in every image picks up a warm cast — measured at `[1.176, 0.976, 0.722]`.
- **⚠⚠ A trap I hit twice while predicting sabotage outcomes: changing ONE direction of a matrix pair always breaks the round trip.** Both times the plan predicted the round trip would survive and it did not, because the sabotage was one-directional. To isolate what a *different* test catches, the sabotage has to keep the pair consistent. The substantive claims held both times.
- **The matrices are fused, not layered.** ProPhoto→XYZ, Bradford and XYZ→LMS are composed offline into one 3×3 rather than shipped as three steps: one multiply per pixel instead of three, and the cross-check validates the whole chain anyway.
- **Still to come — plan 2:** the decode flag (`output_color = 4`), the output transform, the eight primary-dependent stages, re-deriving the five tuned constants, re-authoring the golden charts in sRGB, and regenerating all 38 goldens. ⚠ **All 38 will move**, which removes this project's usual "the others stayed identical" check — the replacement is that the `neutral` golden must stay within `TOL`, because sRGB→ProPhoto→sRGB is a round trip.

**A bundled icon font (2026-08-24).** Phosphor Icons 2.1.2, MIT, vendored at `crates/app/assets/Phosphor.ttf`. The rail and toolbar draw real icons now. **702 tests**; no golden moved.

- **⚠ Why it was needed at all:** egui's bundled fonts — Hack, Ubuntu-Light, NotoEmoji, `emoji-icon-font` — contain **about twenty** usable symbols out of ~120 probed, and **no magnifying glass whatsoever**. The zoom buttons had been `⊞`/`⊟` plus a text `1:1`, which was a workaround rather than a design.
- **⚠⚠ Registered as a FALLBACK via `Context::add_font`, never a hand-built `FontDefinitions`.** Building one from scratch **replaces** the whole stack, which would discard egui's own fonts and leave every ordinary label in the app unrenderable. `FontPriority::Lowest` means the icon font is consulted only for glyphs nothing earlier has — and since Phosphor's codepoints are all Private Use Area, nothing else ever reaches it. The payoff: **call sites stay ordinary text**, `Tool::glyph` still returns a plain `char`, and no `FontId` juggling anywhere.
- **⚠ `icons` is a LIB module**, because `Tool::glyph` needs it and `tool` is in the lib. Every constant names its Phosphor icon in a doc comment — a bare `'\u{e30e}'` is a magic literal that cannot be checked against anything, and the name is what makes a font upgrade auditable.
- **⚠⚠ `every_icon_is_in_the_private_use_area` catches what the coverage tests structurally cannot.** A mistyped constant landing on a *real* character — U+0430, a Cyrillic 'а' — resolves happily in Ubuntu-Light and renders as a letter. `has_glyph` returns **true**, so every coverage test passes and the button quietly shows the wrong thing. Sabotaged with exactly that character and watched to fail.
- **⚠ The two glyph-coverage tests now install the real font stack.** They had been asking a bare `Context::default()`, i.e. egui's defaults — which production no longer uses. Without the install they would fail on every icon *while the app rendered them perfectly*.
- **⚠ A sabotage that legitimately passes, recorded rather than papered over.** Flipping `FontPriority::Lowest` to `Highest` fails nothing: an icon font has no `A`, so the Latin fallback still supplies it. `the_icon_font_is_registered` and `installing_the_icon_font_keeps_the_ordinary_ones` guard **presence**, not priority. `Lowest` is kept anyway — consulting a 1,500-glyph font first for every character is wasted work.
- **⚠ The discriminating sabotage for the coverage test is a codepoint INSIDE the PUA but outside Phosphor's block** (`U+F8FE`). One outside the PUA would fail the range test too and prove nothing about coverage. Verified both ways: range test still green, coverage test failed.
- **⚠ `ZOOM_FIT` is `frame-corners`, not a fourth magnifying glass.** Phosphor has exactly three glass variants — plain, plus, minus. A frame is the conventional fit-to-window symbol anyway, but this is a departure from what was asked for and is recorded as one.
- **⚠ The font is PINNED to 2.1.2 and the codepoints were verified against that version specifically**, not against `@latest` — which is what the pin exists to prevent. An upgrade means re-fetching that version's `style.css` and comparing the eleven `.ph-<name>:before` contents.
- **`LICENSE` now exists at the repo root** (GPL-3.0). `Cargo.toml` had declared `license = "GPL-3.0-or-later"` for the life of the project without the text ever being shipped; vendoring somebody else's font was the moment to fix it. Phosphor's MIT text sits beside the font.
- **⚠ Measured, not estimated: the release binary went 16.7 → 17.13 MB** (+0.43 MB). The first reading said 16.7 and was stale — taken as the process appeared rather than after the link. Confirmed the font is genuinely embedded by finding a chunk from the *middle* of the TTF inside the binary, not merely the name string.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) no subsetting — `fonttools` is not installed, and trading 480 KB for a regeneration step on every new icon is a poor deal at this size; (2) icons inherit the surrounding text colour, so they follow the theme and accent for free but cannot be multi-coloured; (3) the in-window menu bar stays text-only, matching its text menus; (4) only the rail and toolbar changed — every other label is still text.

**Fixes found by using the finished rework (2026-08-24).** Nine of them, in three rounds, every one reported from real use and none by a test. **696 → 699 tests.** ⚠ **This is the pattern this file has recorded since the brush canvas: the pure logic held, and every defect was in wiring.**

**Round 1 — the native menu did not work at all.**

- **⚠⚠ `MenuEvent::set_event_handler(Some(f))` STOPS muda's own `receiver()` from ever firing.** `send` calls the handler *instead of* pushing to the channel, so `poll()` drained a channel that could never receive and **every menu item silently did nothing**. muda's own doc comment says so outright. The handler now forwards the id to our own `mpsc` channel *and* requests a repaint — it needs to do both, for two unrelated reasons. ⚠ **The plan predicted this exact failure and named the symptom**, which is why it took one look at the crate source rather than an afternoon; it was still shipped once, because the plan's verification step was "click Open file…" and the GUI got a green build reported instead.
- **⚠⚠ AppKit ALWAYS treats the first submenu of the main menu as the application menu**, whatever its title says, and relabels it with the app name. "File" was absorbed — Open file, Open folder and Export appeared under "Rustroom" and there was no File menu at all. An explicit app submenu now goes first.
- The top row still carried Open file / Open folder. They were in `shell.rs`, not the develop toolbar that was slimmed.

**Round 2 — macOS conventions and the toolbar icons.**

- **⚠ The application menu's title is the EXECUTABLE NAME** for an unbundled binary — there is no `CFBundleName` — so it read "rustroom-gui". **The bin target is now `Rustroom`**; that is the whole fix. ⚠ `cargo test -p app --bin Rustroom` from here on.
- **⚠ AppKit injects "Emoji & Symbols" and "Start Dictation" into any menu titled "Edit"**, uninvited. Suppressed by registering `NSDisabledDictationMenuItem` / `NSDisabledCharacterPaletteMenuItem`. ⚠ `registerDefaults`, **not** `setBool_forKey` — registering applies to this process, setting would write into the user's own preferences plist. Costs a macOS-only `objc2` + `objc2-foundation` dependency, both already in the tree via muda.
- **Settings moved into the application menu** with ⌘, where Mac users look for it, and is skipped from File there. Same treatment About already had. The model keeps both in their cross-platform places for the in-window bar, which has no app menu.
- **⚠⚠ NO MAGNIFYING GLASS EXISTS in egui's bundled fonts.** 🔍 🔎 ⌕ are all absent, so the requested zoom icons were impossible. Of ~120 characters probed across three batches, only about **twenty** render: `☂ ★ ☆ ☰ ◑ ◔ ◕ ■ ▣ ○ ◎ ⌘ ✚ ⚙ ⟲ ⟳ ⛶ ⊞ ⊟ ± ⌖ ⊗ ▓`. Shipped: undo `⟲`, redo `⟳`, zoom out `⊟`, zoom in `⊞`, fit `⛶`, and **1:1 as text** because nothing suitable exists. `every_toolbar_glyph_is_renderable` guards them the way the rail's guard does. ⚠ **The real fix, if icons matter, is bundling an icon font** — one embedded TTF in `FontDefinitions` removes this constraint permanently, for the rail too.
- **New commands `ZoomIn` / `ZoomOut` / `ZoomActual`**, and the exhaustive dispatcher match refused to compile until each was handled — which is what that match is for. ⚠ `Session.fit_scale` is written by the canvas each frame because **only the canvas knows the viewport**, and "zoom to 1:1" cannot compute it otherwise; it mirrors `fit_image_size`, which exists for the same reason. Clamped through `zoom_step(1.0, target)` rather than a second clamp helper, so the MIN/MAX rule lives in one place.
- **⚠ Two documentation facts were wrong and cost a compile each.** `Modifiers::CMD_OR_CTRL` does not exist — **`CMD_OR_CTRL` is a free const in `muda::accelerator`**, resolving to `SUPER` on macOS. And `Tool` needed `Hash`, because `Command::SelectTool(Tool)` keys the native id map. **Read the vendored crate source, not a docs summary.**

**Round 3 — the top row, and before/after.**

- **⚠⚠ Before/after was rendered ONCE, at preview resolution, by the decode worker** — a texture the canvas then magnified, so at 1:1 it was roughly 4× undersampled. It is a normal render now, at the current rung and region, routed through **`Session::view_edits`**, which **every** request site goes through so none can forget the before state.
- **⚠⚠ "Before" keeps the geometry and drops only the adjustments**, and this is not a preference. A *fully* neutral render is **uncropped**, so it has different dimensions from the edited one — the region and placement maths would then describe different content, and toggling while zoomed would jump elsewhere in the photo. Holding the framing fixed is what makes a zoomed comparison a comparison. `the_before_view_keeps_the_geometry_and_drops_the_adjustments` pins it. Cost: toggling is a render rather than a texture swap, though both keys stay in the prefix cache after the first toggle each way.
- **The format filter is Library-only**, right-aligned, and **multi-select checkboxes**. ⚠ It went to single-select radios with an "All" entry first — **wrong**: that made "RAF and JPG but not PNG" unreachable on a mixed folder, and "All" is redundant when every box ticked says the same thing. The `show_only`/`only_shown` API added for the radios was **removed with them** rather than left as unused surface.
- **⚠ A guard that read backwards.** Disabling the last still-ticked format, to stop the grid going empty, greyed out whichever format was still shown — which from the user's side is "I clicked one and the others went dark". Removed: unticking everything is recoverable in one click because the boxes are still on screen, so the guard cost more than it bought. **A protective disable is only worth it when the state it prevents is hard to leave.**
- **⚠ `shell` owns the row's whole right-aligned cluster now.** The develop toolbar had been right-aligning the filename itself, and the status label was added *after* it — into a row with no space left. Two right-aligned regions in one row is a bug by construction. The filename is no longer in the top row at all: the develop panel's footer already has it.
- **⚠ The status line is per view**, in two parts, because one was not enough: it **clears on a view switch** (one edge check in the frame loop, so the segmented switch, the View menu and the G/D keys are all covered without each remembering), *and* "Full resolution ready" is only raised while Develop is on screen — a full-res decode can finish while the user is browsing the Library.
- **The Library/Develop switch is a segmented control** — zero item spacing between the two halves and padded labels. As separate buttons they read as two unrelated actions; abutted they read as one control with two states, which is what a module switch is.

**Two process notes from this round.**

- **⚠ Tasks 2 and 3 of the menu-bar plan had to land in one commit**: `commands::execute` is a `pub fn` in a **binary** crate with no caller until the key routing exists. **Fifth instance** of that trap, after `theme::button_row`, the CLI's `format_arg`, `ELLIPSE_SEGMENTS` and the panel's `HslChannel`. The rule is unchanged and keeps being relearned: in a bin crate an item lands **with its first caller**.
- **⚠ A test caught an incomplete mapping and the fix was to complete the table, not weaken the test.** `every_shortcut_maps_to_an_egui_key` failed on ⌘C/⌘V, which `pressed_command` deliberately skips. Adding a skip to the *test* would have been the "assertion asserts something false about correct code" trap; instead `egui_key` is a **total** mapping and the skip stays in `pressed_command`, where it is documented. A hole in the table would leave the completeness test unable to mean what it says.
- **⚠ A cleanup that overreached, and was reverted.** Renaming the binary swept `--bin rustroom-gui` through **10 plan documents recording already-executed work**. Those are records; only `CLAUDE.md` and the three layout plans describe how to build the thing now.

**The menu bar — step 3 of 3, and the rework closes with it.** A native NSMenu on macOS and an in-window `egui::MenuBar` elsewhere, both built from one model, with the Develop toolbar falling from ~14 controls to **seven icon buttons**. 688 → **696 tests**; no golden moved. ⚠ **Three bugs in this feature were found straight afterwards by using it** — see the fixes section above, which supersedes some of what follows.

- **⚠⚠ THE SAFETY RULE THAT REPLACED THE SPEC'S DESIGN.** The spec proposed disabling Copy/Paste on the native menu while a text field has focus. That machinery was **not built**; `Shortcut::safe_as_native_accelerator` removes the hazard at its source instead. A native accelerator is consumed *before* winit sees the key, so **a bare key** (`G`, `D`, `\`, `1`–`4`) would steal every such character typed into the Save-preset field, and **a text-editing chord** (⌘Z/C/V/X/A) would break editing inside it. Only ⌘O, ⇧⌘O, ⌘E, ⌘, , ⌘[ , ⌘] are safe. The rest keep egui's existing focus-gated handling and show their chord *in the label*. Less machinery, and nothing to forget on a frame.
- **⚠ The egui menu bar compiles on EVERY platform; only its invocation is gated.** The spec implied `#[cfg]`-ing it to non-macOS — which would mean the developer (on macOS) never compiles it and Linux CI never compiles the muda one, leaving **both halves unverified by whoever is looking**. `native_menu.rs` is the sole gated module and is deliberately thin.
- **⚠ `muda` is a target-specific dependency** (`[target.'cfg(target_os = "macos")'.dependencies]`), so Linux CI never builds it. Its Linux backend needs GTK, which eframe/winit does not provide — that constraint is what forced two renderers in the first place.
- **⚠ Two documentation facts that were wrong and cost a compile each.** `Modifiers::CMD_OR_CTRL` does not exist — `CMD_OR_CTRL` is a **free const in `muda::accelerator`**, resolving to `SUPER` on macOS. And `Tool` needed `Hash` because `Command::SelectTool(Tool)` keys the native id map. **Read the vendored crate source, not a docs summary.**
- **⚠ SUPERSEDED — see the fixes section.** `set_event_handler` *replaces* muda's channel delivery, so the original "`poll` still drains `MenuEvent::receiver()`" was broken and no menu item worked. The handler forwards to our own channel now. The repaint half below is still true and still necessary:
- **⚠ `MenuEvent::set_event_handler` also has to `request_repaint`.** egui does not redraw an idle window and a menu click generates no winit event, so without the repaint the command sits in the channel until the mouse moves — the same class of bug as the full-res trigger needing `request_repaint_after`. The dispatch stays in `poll` because a `'static` closure cannot hold `&mut RustroomApp`.
- **⚠ muda's types are `!Send`/`!Sync` and macOS menus panic off the main thread**, so the menu lives in a `thread_local!` rather than on `RustroomApp` — which keeps `#[cfg]` out of the app struct entirely.
- **⚠ A test caught an incomplete mapping, and the fix was to complete the table rather than weaken the test.** `every_shortcut_maps_to_an_egui_key` failed on ⌘C/⌘V, which `pressed_command` deliberately skips. Adding a skip to the *test* would have been the "assertion asserts something false about correct code" trap; instead `egui_key` is now a **total** mapping and the skip stays in `pressed_command`, where it is documented. A hole in the table would make the completeness test unable to say what it means.
- **⚠ Tasks 2 and 3 had to land in one commit** — `commands::execute` is a `pub fn` in a **binary** crate with no caller until the key routing exists. **Fifth instance** of that trap, after `theme::button_row`, the CLI's `format_arg`, `ELLIPSE_SEGMENTS` and step 1's `HslChannel`.
- **The Keyboard-shortcuts dialog is generated from `menu::model()`**, never hand-written — a reference listing a chord the app does not have is worse than no reference.
- **⚠ BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: `Export…` and `Sync…` lose their dynamic counts.** A menu item cannot carry a label that changes usefully. That count is how the selection-wide scope was discoverable, so this is a real regression, taken knowingly; the count is in the dialogs, which name their targets.
- **⚠ The in-window bar has NEVER BEEN RUN**, only unit-tested — there is no Linux machine here and macOS never shows it. `the_bar_renders_every_section_without_panicking` is the whole of its coverage. Flip the `!cfg!(target_os = "macos")` guard in `shell.rs` to look at it.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) `native_menu.rs` can rot silently, since CI never compiles it; (2) the unsafe chords show as label text rather than native right-aligned accelerators on macOS; (3) no user-defined shortcuts; (4) the Library view's toolbar is unchanged apart from losing what moved into menus.

**Per-tool resets and a lockable crop aspect (2026-08-24).** Follow-ups to the rail, found by using it. **688 tests.**

- **Each tool resets its own edit group and nothing else** — "Reset adjustments" (Edit), "Reset crop" (Crop), "Remove all masks" (Masks). That is what an exclusive panel implies: a tool must not discard a crop or a painted mask the user cannot see from where they are standing. ⚠ The Edit reset's group set is **derived from `SECTIONS`**, and the other two go through named `GEOMETRY_GROUP` / `MASKS_GROUP` constants rather than literal `5` / `6` — a wrong index still compiles and still resets *something*.
- **⚠⚠ The crop aspect is now a lock, and the drive-axis rule took two wrong answers before the right one.** Picking 1:1/3:2/4:3/16:9 locks the ratio; every corner and edge drag preserves it. `crop_tool::apply_drag` gained an `aspect: Option<f32>`. **The bug a test caught:** a corner must drive its size from whichever axis *changed most*, not from width (a straight-down drag then does nothing) and **not from `fw.max(fh * a)`** — that only ever *grows*, so it ignores the drag entirely whenever you are shrinking, which is the common case. `a_locked_corner_follows_a_purely_vertical_drag` is the discriminating test, and it failed against the first implementation.
- **⚠ The lock stores the PIXEL ratio, not the normalized one.** Normalized aspect is `pixel / image_aspect`, so it differs per photo — storing that would silently change the crop's shape the moment you switched to a photo with different dimensions. Converted at the point of use, from `s.baseline`'s aspect, which is the same source the Crop panel's presets use so the two cannot disagree.
- **⚠ An edge drag holds the perpendicular *centre* fixed**, or the rect creeps up the frame as you widen it. A corner holds the opposite corner. Sabotaging the centre anchoring fails `a_locked_edge_drag_keeps_the_ratio_and_the_perpendicular_centre`.
- **⚠ "Free" no longer resets the crop**, it only unlocks. It used to reset to the full frame, which made unlocking destructive; resetting is now "Reset crop"'s job. The presets are `selectable_label`s, because a lock with no shown selection is **invisible state** — you would only discover it by dragging a handle and finding it refuse.
- **⚠ A degenerate aspect is guarded at the door.** `w / a` with `a = 0` is infinity, and the subtraction that follows is NaN; a NaN rect then compares false against every clamp, so the crop would quietly stop responding rather than fail loudly.
- The four pre-existing `crop_tool` tests kept their assertions **verbatim** through a `dragged` helper — the same routing `mask_tool::apply_drag` used when it changed shape — so their passing is the evidence the free path did not move. This closes the M1.4b simplification "aspect presets *snap* the rect (no fully aspect-locked interactive drag)".

**The tool rail — step 2 of 3 of the layout/IA rework.** A four-entry rail (Edit · Crop · Masks · Presets) on the **right edge, outermost**, whose selection **replaces** the panel's contents. 672 → **678 tests**; no golden moved.

- **⚠⚠ THE GLYPH FINDING, and it is the reason to probe rather than assume.** The design spec's mockup used **▦ ⧉ ◐ ≡**. Every one of those is **absent from egui 0.35's bundled fonts** (Hack, Ubuntu-Light, NotoEmoji, `emoji-icon-font`) and would have rendered as a tofu box — invisible to every other test in the suite. Found by probing `Fonts::has_glyph` *before* writing the plan. **Measured present:** `☂ ★ ☆ ☰ ◑ ◔ ◕ ■ ▣ ○ ◎ ⌘ ✚ ⚙`. **Shipped:** Edit `☰`, Crop `▣`, Masks `◑`, Presets `★`. `every_rail_glyph_is_renderable` is now the guard, sabotaged with `◐` and watched to fail.
- **⚠ `Fonts::has_glyph` needs a frame to have run.** `Context::default()` has not built its atlas, so asking a fresh context answers about nothing. The test runs one empty `run_ui` first.
- **⚠⚠ THE PANEL IS EXCLUSIVE, and this reverses the brainstorm's own choice.** The design picked Lightroom Classic's shape — a tool *drawer above a permanent adjustment stack*, so the global sliders stayed reachable while masking. **Reversed the same day, after using it**: mixing a tool's controls with the global stack made it ambiguous which of the two a slider belonged to. Global edits now live **only under `Edit`**. A mask already carries its own Temp…Vibrance, so the Masks tool never needed them — which was the argument for exclusivity at the brainstorm and turned out to be right. ⚠ Consequences: `Reset all` moved under `Edit` (under Masks it reads as "reset this mask", which it is not), and the tool heading is a **plain heading, not a `CollapsingHeader`** — collapsing the only content would leave an empty panel.
- **⚠ Rail typography had to be fixed in use, and the cause is worth knowing.** The first version was a 64 pt rail with a `"{glyph}\n{label}"` string at the compact body size. Three faults at once: the two rows are **left-aligned against each other**, so a one-character glyph sits hard left of a seven-character word; the label was too small; and "Presets" wrapped onto a second line. Now **88 pt**, a **22 pt** glyph over a **13 pt** label, built from a `LayoutJob` with **`halign: Center`** — which centres each row against the widest — and `TextWrapMode::Extend`. ⚠ The surrounding `vertical_centered_justified` centres the *block*, because `AtomLayout` takes its default `align2` from `ui.layout()`; the `LayoutJob` centres the *rows within it*. Both are needed and they do different jobs.
- **⚠ The rail's click test builds its entry through the same `entry_text` production uses.** When the widget changed from `selectable_label` to a `Button` with a `LayoutJob`, a harness that had re-declared the old string would still have passed while testing a widget that no longer shipped — the hit rect is what the test is about.
- **⚠ `Session::geometry_mode` is DELETED — derived from `Tool::is_geometry()`.** Two facts that could disagree became one, the same correction `render_max_dim` needed. **Thirteen read sites**, all named by the compiler. The rule at each: **hoist the tool into a local before borrowing the session** — `self.tool` is an immutable borrow of `self` and `&mut self.session` a mutable one, so reading it afterwards is `E0502`. `Tool` is `Copy`, so the hoist is free.
- **⚠ `preview_edits` takes a `Tool`, not a `bool`.** A bare `bool` at a call site is exactly the parameter that gets passed inverted with nothing to notice.
- **⚠ BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: the tool survives a photo switch.** It lives on `RustroomApp`, not `Session`, so arrowing to the next photo keeps you in Crop or Masks. Geometry mode used to reset. Deliberate, and what a rail implies.
- **⚠ BEHAVIOUR CHANGE: mask handles and brush painting are exclusive to the Masks tool.** Previously the mask overlay drew in *any* non-geometry mode, so a stray drag could paint a stroke. The canvas now matches on the tool exhaustively — `Tool::Edit | Tool::Presets => {}` — so a fifth tool forces a decision.
- **⚠⚠ A near-miss worth recording: converting `if geometry { .. } else { .. }` into a `match` almost enabled zoom/pan in Crop mode.** The zoom/pan block sat in the `else`, i.e. "not geometry". Turning the branch into a per-tool match left it in a bare block, silently applying to every tool — and the crop overlay is placed by *fit-mode* maths (the rung is pinned at `fit_cap` in geometry mode), so panning underneath it would misplace it. Now explicitly `if !tool.is_geometry()`, with a comment saying it was the `else`. **When replacing an if/else with a match, check what else lived in each branch.**
- **⚠ `rail::show` returns nothing, on purpose.** `body` detects a change by comparing against the previous frame's tool, which covers the **1–4 keyboard shortcuts** as well; a click-only return value would let a keypress switch tools without re-rendering. The re-render matters because `preview_edits` strips the crop in geometry mode — without it the crop overlay draws over a *cropped* preview, structurally the M3.1a `draw_mask_overlay` bug.
- **⚠ Presets is drawn OUTSIDE the panel's mutable session borrow**, and this is not stylistic: `presets::show` needs `&mut RustroomApp` while `s` already holds `&mut app.session` — not disjoint fields, unlike the five `&mut` in `masks::BrushTool`. It is the one drawer that edits neither `Session` nor `Edits`, so the histogram takes a *shared* borrow that ends, Presets runs, then the session is borrowed mutably for everything else.
- **Presets now has exactly one home** — the left panel, the `show_presets` field and the toolbar toggle are all gone. So is the Geometry toggle.
- **⚠ `Tool` lives in the LIB, and could therefore be committed alone** — a `pub` item in a lib is public API, so `dead_code` does not fire. That is the exact contrast with step 1's `HslChannel`, which is a bin module and had to land with its caller.
- **⚠ `draw_mask_overlay` already had a parameter called `tool`** (the `BrushTool` settings). Renamed to `brush`, matching `masks.rs`. A collision like that is a good prompt to check whether the older name was ever the right one.
- **Panel order is now rail (right, outermost) → adjustments (right) → filmstrip (bottom) → canvas (central)**, documented on `develop::body`. The rail coming *first* is what puts it against the window edge rather than between the panel and the image.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) the `#[cfg]`-gated macOS menu work is still step 3, so the toolbar is not yet slimmed; (2) no rail reordering or customisation; (3) the 1–4 keys are handled locally and move into the `Command` dispatcher in step 3; (4) tool selection is not persisted across restarts.

**The panel's sections are the edit groups — step 1 of 3 of the layout/IA rework.** Thirteen always-open ungrouped sliders plus seven `CollapsingHeader`s became **five collapsible sections named by `EDIT_GROUP_NAMES`**. 664 → **672 tests**; **no golden moved**, which is the proof this touched no pipeline code.

- **⚠⚠ The sections are *read from* `EDIT_GROUP_NAMES`, never hardcoded.** `Section(usize)` indexes that table, so the panel teaches the same five words the Paste, Sync and Save-preset dialogs already use, and renaming a group updates the header for free. Geometry and Masks are the two groups the panel does **not** show — they become rail tools in step 2 — and `geometry_and_masks_are_the_two_groups_the_panel_does_not_show` states that as a property over the whole list, so a *seventh* group cannot silently go missing.
- **⚠ The Lightroom-style `Basic` grouping was rejected**, and the reason is the point of the whole change: "Basic" spans three edit groups, so the panel and the Sync dialog would name the same controls differently — the exact ambiguity being removed everywhere else. Accepted cost: a two-slider White Balance section and a nine-slider Tone one.
- **⚠ Panel order deliberately does NOT follow pipeline order**, and this is now written into `SECTIONS`' doc comment so nobody "fixes" it. The pipeline runs NR and dehaze *before* white balance because that is where the maths wants them; NR is *judged* last, at 1:1, once tone is settled. Panel follows workflow, pipeline follows maths.
- **⚠ `Edited` replaced the `slider` closure, and that is what made the split possible at all.** The old shape was one `FnMut` capturing `changed`/`settled` for 350 lines, so any *second* closure touching either was `E0499`. **Two comments in the file existed solely to describe working around that** — the `mask_changed` indirection and the brush copy-into-locals-and-write-back — and **both are now deleted rather than carried along**. A `&mut Edited` crosses a function boundary, which a capture cannot.
- **⚠ `masks::BrushTool` is five `&mut` to *disjoint* fields of `RustroomApp`.** Likewise `adjustments::show` takes `hsl_channel` as its own parameter while the caller holds `&mut app.session`. Disjoint field borrows are legal; it was only ever the *closure capture* that was not.
- **Five ordering changes, all agreed rather than inherited:** HSL became a `Hue│Saturation│Luminance` selector instead of three nested `CollapsingHeader`s (which cost a third nesting level *and* put a "Saturation" header under a "Saturation" slider meaning something else); **Vibrance above Saturation** (Adobe's order, gentler tool first); **Noise Reduction above Sharpening** — ⚠ a *deliberate* divergence from Lightroom, on the grounds that you must denoise before you can judge sharpening; a hairline before Texture/Clarity/Dehaze inside Tone; and a mask's adjustments now mirror the global order.
- **⚠ Masks moved to the TOP of the panel, above White Balance** — already its final position, since step 2 turns it into a rail drawer that opens exactly there. That step only has to move its trigger.
- **⚠ The plan was wrong about one thing, and the binary-crate dead-code rule is why.** It made "add `HslChannel`" its own task with its own commit, expecting clippy clean. But `ALL`, `label`, `value_mut` and the new `hsl_channel` field have no *production* caller until the sections exist, so `-D warnings` rejects all four as dead code. **Tasks 3 and 4 had to land in one commit.** This is the fourth instance of that trap after `theme::button_row`, the CLI's `format_arg` and `ELLIPSE_SEGMENTS` — the rule is: in a bin crate, an item lands **with** its first caller, always.
- **⚠ The `*name` deref broke on the way through.** The old `slider` closure had an inferred label type, so band names needed `*name`; `Edited::slider` declares `label: &str`, which auto-derefs, and clippy's `explicit_auto_deref` caught all three sites. A typed parameter is stricter than an inferred one.
- **⚠ `clicking_a_segment_switches_the_channel` is the test that matters**, because `value_mut` being right proves nothing about the segments being *wired* to it. **Sabotaged by collapsing the harness from three passes to one, and watched to fail** — that is the ninth-instance guard against the `a_left_drag_does_not_pan` failure mode, where the assertion was right and the harness fed it zeros. Every channel is clicked, not just one, because a selector stuck on its default would pass a Hue-only test.
- **Every new test was sabotaged with a compile check first**, per M3.1b's process finding. All four sabotages compiled cleanly and all four were caught.
- **File sizes:** `panel.rs` 392 → `mod.rs` 184 + `adjustments.rs` 297 + `masks.rs` 149 + `geometry.rs` 72.
- **Still to come — this is step 1 of 3.** Step 2: the rail, drawers, Crop/Masks/Presets as tools, the left preset panel and Geometry toggle removed, `geometry_mode` becoming derived. Step 3: the menu bar, both renderers, the toolbar slimming to six controls. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-23-layout-ia-rework-design.md`.

**Region-scoped coverage is complete — the brush renders live while you paint.** Coverage is rasterized over the **rendered region** rather than the whole frame. 664 tests; **no golden moved**, which is the whole safety argument.

- **⚠⚠ It REVERSES `PrefixCache`'s rule, deliberately: the region is now IN `BrushCache`'s key.** That rule — region out of the key so panning never invalidates — is right for the prefix, which runs whole-buffer and whose output *is* the frame. It was copied to the brush without being re-derived, and there it buys a cache that survives a pan (invisible, since panning re-runs the whole tail anyway) and charges a full-frame rasterization on every paint frame. **Both cache sites now carry a comment saying why they differ — do not "unify" them.**
- **⚠ The second reason the region belongs in the key is that it makes a clip mismatch unrepresentable.** `MaskStage` derives its clip from `Region` plus the incoming image's dims; `render_engine` derives it from `RegionRect`. A mismatch would not error — it would read `outside` across the difference, fading the mask out partway across the image.
- **⚠⚠ The spec predicted ~1.6 ms for clipped rasterization and was wrong by 5×.** Measured on an X-T4 at 1:1: **24.64 ms unclipped → 9.03 ms clipped**, plus 7.2 ms region tail and ~0.4 ms pack, so **a paint frame is ~16.6 ms**. The clip bounds the **box**, but every dab *inside* it still costs its full area, and at 1:1 a dab is 125 px across. The estimate came from extrapolating the fit-resolution number, where dabs are small in absolute pixels. ⚠ **The remaining cost is dab area, not frame area** — so if this ever needs to be faster, incremental splatting is the lever, now for a well-understood 9 ms.
- **⚠ 16.6 ms is marginal against a 60 Hz budget and is recorded as such**, not claimed as comfortable. It was confirmed smooth in use, which is why it shipped; the spec fixed that band in advance so the decision was made against a number.
- **⚠ `drag_started` stays suppressed even with live rendering on.** Rendering a one-point stroke you are about to extend caused the "initial dot" bug — a full-resolution render landing after the line was finished and painting over it. **Live rendering means rendering on *motion*, never on press.**
- **⚠ The clip must be `RegionRect`'s own `w`/`h`, never "frame minus origin".** The latter clips to the frame's bottom-right corner rather than the viewport — larger than intended and **silently so**, since the output stays correct and merely slow.
- **⚠ The dab skip is an optimisation, not a correctness guard** — the plan pre-flagged that its sabotage might legitimately pass, and it did. The box is clamped to the clip regardless, so an out-of-clip dab contributes nothing either way; what the skip saves is the box widening to the stroke's full extent. The test guards the *output*, and says so.
- **`FrameMap` is untouched**, so dab positions and the pre-geometry anchoring guarantee are exactly as 3.1c-A left them. `outside`/`is_inert` are unaffected, so the inverted-mask bug cannot return through the clip.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) panning at 1:1 with a brush mask selected now misses the brush cache — the trade this makes, and not reported as worse; (2) two caches with opposite rules about the region; (3) no incremental splatting, so a very large stroke still re-rasterizes its visible part each frame; (4) the tint is still a second rasterization, unchanged and deliberate.

**M3.1c-B is complete — brush strokes are authorable on the canvas, and M3.1 is closed.** Painting, the brush cursor, the coverage tint, panel controls, a coverage cache, and **flow**, pulled in from a later milestone. 655 tests, 38 goldens.

- **No paint mode**, and that is the payoff of sequencing the navigation rework first: left-drag was deliberately freed, so a drag on a selected brush mask simply paints.
- **⚠⚠ SIX BUGS, EVERY ONE FOUND IN USE AND NONE BY THE TESTS.** This is the most important thing in this section. The pure functions — `FrameMap`, `inverse_point`, the rasterizer, decimation — carry ~30 tests and produced **not one defect**; every bug was in the **canvas wiring**, which has almost no coverage. Headless `RawInput` tests of the drag lifecycle would have caught at least three of them. **Treat canvas/panel work as untested territory until that changes.**

  | bug | cause |
  |---|---|
  | wgpu crash, 711 MB vertex buffer | tint drawn as a mesh — millions of quads across a soft gradient |
  | the softest brush still painted at full strength | `hardness` shapes an *edge*; nothing controlled strength |
  | invert covered only a rectangle around the strokes | `CoverageMap` had no value for outside its bbox |
  | a drawn line rendered as its first dab only | `drag_started` kicked off a full-res render of a one-point stroke, which landed late and painted over the good one |
  | sluggish at 1:1 only with the mask shown | the tint rasterized at `frame_rect`, which at 1:1 is the **whole frame**, not the viewport |
  | the tint drifted with the window when panning | the cache stored a **screen rect**, so a hit survived a pan |

- **⚠⚠ The last one is a REGRESSION of a bug this project had already fixed.** 3.1a's mask overlay drifted while panning because it was placed against `img_rect` rather than the frame; the fix made every coordinate frame-relative and is recorded in this file. Caching a *derived screen position* quietly undid it. **The rule: cache the expensive thing, never the placement.**
- **⚠ Flow should never have been deferred, and deferring it produced an unusable brush.** `hardness` shapes a dab's edge — even at hardness 0 the centre reaches full coverage — so the softest possible brush painted at full strength, and because dabs combined with a flat `max`, a second pass changed nothing. Both complaints reduce to a missing strength control. **The composition rule turned out cleaner than the deferral feared: per-stroke `max`, then composite across strokes** (`cov += (1 − cov) · a · flow`). One pass stays uniform; repeated passes build up. That two-level rule is why dabs are grouped per stroke rather than flattened.
- **⚠ All 37 goldens stayed byte-identical through the flow change, and that was a GAP not a comfort.** `mask_brush_exposure` has one stroke and `mask_brush_erase` uses a hard-edged eraser — cases where `max` and compositing agree exactly. A model change no reference can see has no regression guard, so `mask_brush_flow_buildup` was added: three overlapping passes at flow 35.
- **⚠ The tint is capped at 1400 px and stretched by the GPU**, because `frame_rect` is the *whole frame's* on-screen rect — at 1:1 that is ~6246×4170, so "rasterize at screen resolution" was rasterizing at full resolution. Cost is now constant in zoom.
- **⚠ One textured quad, never a mesh.** Run-length quads were defended as sufficient and are not: they collapse a stroke's flat interior, but across a *soft* gradient every run is length 1, and a large soft stroke is mostly gradient. The optimisation helped exactly where the cost was not.
- **⚠ The pipeline renders once per stroke, on release — not per drag frame.** At 1:1 a per-frame render was ~24 ms before the tail and pack. The tint carries the live feedback. ⚠ **`drag_stopped` must live OUTSIDE `if let Some(p) = resp.interact_pointer_pos()`** — on the release frame egui often reports no interact position, and nesting it there silently skipped both the render and the history push.
- **⚠ `CoverageMap` carries an `outside` value**, so an inverted brush covers the whole frame while the box still spans only what was painted. `is_empty` became `is_inert` — an inverted brush with no strokes covers *everything*, and a box-only test would call it inert and skip a stage that must run.
- **⚠ `BrushCache::CAP = 2`, for the reason `PrefixCache::FINALS_CAP` is 2**: frame dims are in the key, so one entry would be evicted on every zoom change. And the coverage index is the **position in `edits.masks`**, not a counter over active masks — an inactive mask still owns a slot.
- **⚠ `FrameMap::inverse_point` is guarded twice, and the second guard earned its place empirically.** Flipping the rotation sign in *both* directions leaves them mutually inverse, so the round trip passes cleanly while agreement with `GeometryStage` fails. A round trip alone cannot detect a forward/inverse pair wrong in mirrored ways.
- **⚠ Instrumentation beat reasoning twice, and both times it was reached for late.** The "first dab only" bug survived three readings of the drag code *because that code was correct*. One `RUSTROOM_DEBUG_BRUSH` line — `14 strokes, last has 42 points, max_dim 6246` — reframed it instantly as a stale render. The env-gated print is kept.
- **⚠ RECOMMENDED FOLLOW-UP: region-scoped coverage, NOT incremental splatting.** Painting still defers the pixel effect to release; Lightroom shows it live because its Develop view renders at *screen* resolution, so its masking work is bounded by the viewport. Rustroom rasterizes coverage **whole-frame** so the region stays out of the cache key and panning never invalidates — free panning, expensive painting. **Rasterizing over the rendered region instead is ~1.6 ms rather than ~24 ms**, cheap enough to render live per drag frame, and panning already re-runs the whole tail anyway. It is *less* machinery than incremental splatting and helps every render at zoom, not just the in-progress stroke.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) the pixel effect appears on release, not during the drag — see the follow-up above; (2) strokes are rasterized twice, by the canvas for the tint and by the worker for the render, deliberately, for responsiveness; (3) no auto-mask/edge detection; (4) no `[`/`]` brush-size shortcuts; (5) tool state is not persisted, because a screen-point size is view-dependent; (6) a stray click paints a dab; (7) no mask intersection or subtraction between masks — `erase` works only within one brush.

**M3.1c-A is complete — the brush's whole model, headless.** Vector strokes in `Edits`, rasterized into a bbox-bounded 8-bit coverage map, anchored in **pre-geometry source coordinates**. 635 tests, goldens 35 → 37, the 35 byte-identical. ⚠ **M3.1 closes with 3.1c-B**, which owes painting, the cursor, the tint, panel controls and the cache.

- **⚠⚠ A brush is not a fourth analytic shape, and this is the structural content of the whole milestone.** `Mask::coverage(u, v, aspect)` is a **pure function of position**; a brush is **sampled data**. So `MaskStage` resolves a mask to a `CoverageSource` — `Analytic(Mask)` or `Painted(Brush, Geometry)` — **once at construction**, and matches on it outside the pixel loop. `Mask::coverage` became **`analytic_coverage(..) -> Option<f32>`**, `None` for a brush: a `Mask::Brush => 0.0` arm would compile, run, and silently produce a mask covering nothing.
- **⚠⚠ Strokes are pre-geometry; the analytic masks stay frame-relative. The asymmetry is principled, not a hack.** `MaskStage` runs in the **tail**, after `GeometryStage`, so frame-normalised coordinates slide over the image content whenever the crop changes. Invisible for a gradient, tolerable for a radial, **ruinous for a stroke painted onto a subject**. A gradient is *compositional* ("darken the top" should stay at the top after a re-crop); a stroke is *content-referred* (it must stay on the cat). Existing sidecars are untouched, which is the second reason not to convert all three.
- **⚠ Radius is a fraction of the source LONG EDGE — the aspect trap in its fourth guise.** Points are normalised per axis, so that space is anisotropic; a radius against *width* compared against *height* makes every dab an ellipse. ⚠ Note this is resolved by **choosing an isotropic unit**, where `LinearGradient::coverage` and `mask_tool::hit_test` need a *correction* and `RadialGradient::coverage` needs **none at all**. Three masks, three different resolutions of one underlying problem.
- **⚠ The bounding box is the representation, not an optimisation bolted on.** A full-frame coverage map at full resolution is 6246×4170 = **26 MB per mask**. Bounding to the painted region makes memory proportional to what was painted *and* gives `MaskStage` the early-out deferred at 3.1a and again at 3.1b. One decision closed both.
- **⚠⚠ `FrameMap` is tested against `GeometryStage` itself** — mark a source pixel, run the real stage, find where it landed — **not against a re-derivation**. `GeometryStage` is written entirely as *inverse* maps because that is what resampling needs; the rasterizer needs the forward direction, so all three steps had to be inverted. Re-deriving that in a test proves only that the same mistake was made twice.
- **⚠ It needs no absolute pre-geometry dimensions, which is why no pipeline signature changed.** The four steps are: orientation (a dimensionless normalised permutation), rotation (needs only the **aspect**, working in units of the oriented image's height to stay isotropic), crop (normalised), × frame dims. And the aspect derives exactly from the frame dims and the crop rect. So only `MaskStage::new`/`new_in_region` gained a `Geometry` argument; **`build_pipeline`, `build_pipeline_split` and `build_pipeline_split_in_region` kept their signatures**, so the CLI, export and every golden compiled untouched.
- **⚠ `Mask` and `MaskedAdjustment` are no longer `Copy`** — a stroke list cannot be. Both stay `Clone`. `mask_tool::apply_drag` became **`&mut Mask` in place**: the by-value form would clone an entire stroke list on every frame of every drag. Its 21 tests kept their assertions *identical*, routed through a `dragged` helper.
- **⚠ Dabs combine with `max`, never accumulation** — within *and* across strokes; erase is `min(c, 1−dab)`, processed in order so erase-after-paint removes and paint-after-erase restores. **This is exactly why flow is a later milestone**: accumulation needs per-stroke bookkeeping so consecutive overlapping dabs do not re-darken, and buys nothing until flow exists.
- **⚠ `HARDNESS_EPS` is a fast path, NOT a NaN guard, and the spec claimed otherwise.** `0.0/0.0` needs exactly `q == 1 && hard == 1`, and the `q >= 1.0` early return already excludes `q == 1`; for `q < 1` with `hard == 1` the division gives `+inf`, clamping to the correct `1.0`. Verified by deletion. ⚠ `RadialGradient::FEATHER_EPS` **genuinely is** load-bearing, because its ramp has no equivalent early return — the comment draws the contrast so nobody "unifies" them.
- **⚠ `MaskStage`'s `is_empty` early return is likewise an optimisation, not a correctness guard.** An empty coverage map reads 0 everywhere and blending at 0 is bit-exactly the identity. The plan predicted this and the sabotage confirmed it.
- **⚠ `hardness` is arithmetically `100 − feather`** in `RadialGradient`'s maths. It keeps the brush word because that is what every brush calls it. **Do not unify them** — the inversion would be silent.
- **Validated on a real X-T4 `.RAF`, cropped**, which is the only case that can falsify the anchoring claim. The same stroke with and without a crop lands at source fraction **(0.358, 0.394) in both** — identical to 0.0008 — while sitting at a *different frame position* (0.358 vs 0.225 horizontally). ⚠ A stroke on an *uncropped* photo would have proved nothing, the same shape as the crop-factor bug that was invisible on 400 uncropped files and total on the one cropped one.
- **⚠ The exposure golden covers the chart's LEFT half only, decided by measuring.** Each band is a 0..1 ramp, so the right end already sits at 253 and any positive lift clips: a full-width stroke pinned 15 of 64 samples. Confined to the darker half, mask-induced clipping is **zero** and 33 samples discriminate.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no cache — every render rasterizes**, fine for the CLI and not for a slider drag; 3.1c-B fixes it; (2) **no way to author a stroke** except by hand-editing a sidecar, the cost of the split and the same one 3.1a paid; (3) **no flow**, deferred to a later milestone — ⚠ the roadmap file in the Obsidian vault still needs that note; (4) per-pixel adjustments only; (5) no mask intersection or subtraction — `erase` works only within one brush; (6) 8-bit coverage; (7) **M3.1 does not close here**.

**Panning moved off left-drag — scroll, two-finger swipe, or a configurable drag button.** Zoom moved to pinch and ctrl+scroll. 602 tests; **no golden moved and nothing outside `crates/app` was touched**. Done as its own change *before* M3.1c, because it alters navigation for every photo and a panning regression would otherwise hide inside a large brush diff.

- **⚠⚠ It could not be a pure addition, and this is the fact that shaped the whole change.** On a macOS trackpad a **two-finger swipe *is* `smooth_scroll_delta`** — the very value `develop/canvas.rs` was reading to zoom. So "add two-finger pan" and "keep scroll-to-zoom" were the same event and could not coexist; zoom had to move to `i.zoom_delta()` in the same commit.
- **⚠ `zoom_delta()` is a multiplicative factor that is 1.0 when idle, not a delta.** Treating it as a delta collapses the zoom to nothing on every idle frame. `an_idle_gesture_does_not_change_the_zoom` exists solely to say so. It folds trackpad pinch *and* ctrl+scroll, which is what lets mouse users keep a zoom gesture once plain scroll is spoken for.
- **⚠⚠ egui reports `dragged()` for EVERY button, not just the primary one.** `interaction.rs` matches `PointerEvent::Pressed { .. }` and **ignores the button** when setting `potential_drag_id`. Harmless while nothing else used those buttons; the moment panning moved onto one, an ungated crop corner would be dragged by the pan gesture. Tool drags are now gated through `tool_drag`/`tool_drag_started`, landed as **its own commit before** the remap. Confirmed empirically, not just by reading the source.
- **⚠⚠ A re-entrant lock that panics, caught by a test.** `pan_delta` first took `&egui::InputState` — but `Response::dragged_by` calls `ctx.input(..)` internally, and holding the lock across it panics in epaint's mutex. **It hides in casual testing**: `dragged_by` is `dragged() && ctx.input(..)`, and `dragged()` reads a plain flag, so the `&&` short-circuits and never takes the second lock *unless something is actually being dragged*. It would have crashed on the first real middle-drag. **Rule: never pass `&InputState` into anything that also receives a `Response`.**
- **⚠⚠ A harness bug made the most important test vacuous — the eighth instance of the standing lesson, and the first caused by the harness rather than the assertion.** `drag_delta()` is per-frame; the harness moved the pointer once on pass 2 and read on pass 3, so it read `[0,0]` and **`a_left_drag_does_not_pan` would have passed with the gating deleted entirely.** ⚠ Found by **instrumenting**, not re-reading: one `eprintln` printing `dragged/down/delta` showed `delta=[0,0]` immediately. Same lesson as the fit-view investigation — reach for a print when the code reads correctly and the behaviour disagrees.
- **⚠ The scroll sign is settled by egui's docs, not by trial.** `Event::MouseWheel`'s delta "indicates how to move the *content* being viewed … positive values, the content should move to the right and down". Panning is moving the content, so `pan += delta`. Recorded because flipping signs until it feels right leaves nothing behind.
- **⚠ A behaviour fix rode along:** the pan-at-fit reset sat *inside* the scroll branch, so a left-drag at fit really could push a fitting image off-centre into grey with nothing putting it back until the next scroll. Unconditional now.
- **`Settings.pan_drag`, three values** — middle-drag (default), right-drag, Space+left-drag. ⚠ **Scroll and two-finger swipe always pan and are deliberately NOT configurable**: they are the universal route, and making them optional would let someone lock themselves out of panning entirely. ⚠ Configurable *now* not for urgency — trackpad and Magic Mouse users are already served by scroll — but because **the pan branch is written exactly once**: against a small enum it costs ~5 lines more than a hardcoded button, where retrofitting means rewriting shipped behaviour plus the settings row anyway. ⚠ `SpaceLeft` is gated on `memory().focused()`, or a space typed into the preset-name field arms panning.
- **⚠ The toolbar hint names the *configured* binding** via `PanDrag::label()`, never the word "middle" literally — otherwise it becomes a lie the moment the setting changes. Same shape as `Mask::label()`.
- **Two pure functions in `view_region`** (`zoom_step`, `zoom_anchor_shift`) taking **plain tuples, not `egui::Vec2`** — that module's own doc says it is deliberately egui-free so it can be tested without a window. ⚠ `zooming_out_shifts_the_opposite_way` exists so that negating the whole anchor expression cannot pass.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **a habit change felt on every photo** — the point, not a side effect; (2) **two-finger swipe at fit does nothing**, because there is nothing to reveal, and it is the caveat most likely to be reported as a bug; (3) no kinetic/inertial panning; (4) three bindings, not general rebinding; (5) the hint is Develop-only; (6) **ctrl+scroll rather than cmd+scroll** on macOS, inherited from egui.

**M3.1b is complete — a radial gradient mask, and the verdict is that 3.1a's architecture held.** 582 tests, goldens 33 → 35, and the 33 came out byte-identical. ⚠ **M3.1 still does not close until 3.1c's brush.**

- **⚠⚠ The aspect correction does NOT come back, and that is the load-bearing finding.** `LinearGradient::coverage` needs an `aspect` because a projection needs a metric, and anisotropic scaling destroys *perpendicularity*. It does **not** destroy *axis-alignment*: an axis-aligned ellipse maps to an axis-aligned ellipse, so `((u−cx)/rx)² + ((v−cy)/ry)² ≤ 1` is already correct on screen with no correction whatsoever. **`RadialGradient::coverage` therefore takes `aspect` and ignores it** — guarded by `radial_coverage_is_independent_of_aspect`, whose only job is to stop a future reader "fixing" the omission that three sibling functions genuinely need.
- **⚠ Rotation is precisely what would bring the trap back** — a rotated ellipse under anisotropic scaling is still an ellipse but with different axes *and a different angle*, so a stored angle would not be the angle you see. That is the main argument for axis-aligned only, ahead of the extra handle and the extra field. Revisit only with that cost understood.
- **⚠ `mask_tool::hit_test` still needs the aspect**, because grabbing a handle is a screen-space distance test. So the two functions differ deliberately and each says so in its doc. **Do not "unify" them.**
- **Inside is covered; `invert` flips it.** Diverges from Lightroom, whose radial filter affects the outside by default — rejected because a new mask that does nothing where you drew it surprises anyone who has not used Lightroom.
- **⚠ `invert` had never had any UI.** It has existed on `LinearGradient` since 3.1a, is honoured by `coverage`, survives drags and is tested — but no control existed, so it was reachable only by hand-editing a sidecar. Survivable for a gradient (drag the endpoints past each other); **not** survivable for a radial, where the subject vignette depends on it and no drag substitutes. A shared checkbox now serves both, driven by `Mask::invert_mut() -> &mut bool`.
- **Feather is a 0–100 slider with a smoothstep ramp**, reusing the vignette's convention so the app has one feather idiom. ⚠ The linear gradient has **no** feather field — its ramp width *is* the distance between its handles — so this is a real asymmetry, not an oversight. Smoothstep rather than linear because a linear ramp in radial distance leaves a visible crease ring.
- **⚠ `RadialGradient`'s `Default` is hand-written where `LinearGradient`'s is derived.** Container-level `#[serde(default)]` fills a missing field from the **struct's** `Default`, so a derived one would give every partial sidecar a zero-radius point-ellipse — and `feather: 50` cannot come from `f32::default()` under any arrangement. Radius `[0.25, 0.25]` renders as an ellipse *similar to the frame*, since both components are fractions of their own axis.
- **⚠ The zero-radius guard returns 0.0, where the linear's returns 1.0.** The linear's choice was between full coverage and NaN; here the honest answer exists — an ellipse with no area covers nothing — and it falls through the invert, so an inverted point-ellipse covers everything. That is the limit of the surrounding behaviour, not a special case.
- **One flat `Handle` enum, and `hit_test`/`apply_drag` now take `Mask` rather than a shape.** `Session.active_mask_handle` is a single field that must hold either, and nesting (`Handle { Linear(..), Radial(..) }`) would **not** make a mismatched pair unrepresentable — only add a level. So `_ => m` covers the pair that cannot occur, and degrading to "the drag does nothing" is the right failure for a UI. ⚠ **3.1c will test this decision much harder**; see the NEXT ACTION note.
- **Five handles; an edge drag resizes about a fixed centre**, and the centre is hit-tested **before** the edges so a small ellipse can still be moved rather than only resized. Radii clamp to `[0.01, 2.0]` and the centre to `[0, 1]` — a shape dragged out of reach cannot be got back.
- **⚠ The plan's sequencing dissolved two of its own predictions, and the lesson generalises.** `mask_tool` was retargeted at `Mask` and `paint_mask` was split out of `draw_mask_overlay` **before** the variant existed, as pure refactors provable by unchanged tests. So the "three wanted compile errors" arrived as **one** (`paint_mask`'s match), and the irrefutable `let Mask::Linear(g) = m.mask` binding had already been deleted rather than becoming an error. **Preparing a seam converts a compile error into a match arm, which is a better place to make the decision.** The predicted `E0499` borrow trap also never fired: it needs two closures over the same local, which was 3.1a's shape, not this one.
- **⚠ `paint_mask` was split on *painting*, not on the whole function** — the drag plumbing (hit-test, apply, history push, `s.request`) stays written once. That is what makes the radial inherit **both** of 3.1a's real-use bug fixes structurally: the `s.request` call after a drag, and placement against `frame_rect` rather than `img_rect`. Duplicating the plumbing per shape is exactly how those two would come back.
- **The ellipse is a 64-point closed polyline** (`egui::Shape::closed_line`); egui has no ellipse primitive. ⚠ `ELLIPSE_SEGMENTS` had to land in the *same commit as its caller* — a `const` with no caller in a **binary** crate is dead code that fails `clippy -D warnings`, the third instance of that trap after `theme::button_row` and the CLI's `format_arg`.
- **Zero change** in `pipeline`, `MaskStage`, `EditGroups`, `merge_from`, the sidecar format, `edit_clipboard`, sync, presets or batch export. That is the evidence the architecture held, and the byte-identical 33 goldens are the proof.
- **⚠ Three tests could not detect what they named, plus a clipping golden** — all four in the standing-lesson list above. Read them; M3.1b produced half of that list's instances in one feature.
- **Validated on a real X-T4 `.RAF`, both directions, measured rather than squinted at.** Inverted at −1.5 EV: centre ratio **1.000**, corners and edges **0.52–0.60**. ⚠ **0.58 is *full* application, not partial** — −1.5 EV is ×0.354 in linear light, which sRGB-encodes to ≈0.61, so reading the linear figure off an encoded file would look like the mask was half working. Non-inverted at +1.2 EV and +60 saturation: the ellipse reads **1.483×** and every probe outside it is **1.000**, unchanged to the byte.
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) **no rotation** — the trade that keeps `coverage` correction-free; (2) **no inner feather guide on the canvas**, so where the ramp *starts* is numeric only (a dashed inner ellipse is a contained follow-up); (3) one clone and full stage runs per mask even where coverage is zero, inherited from 3.1a — ⚠ radial is where a bounding-box optimisation would start to pay, and it is now deferred twice; (4) per-pixel adjustments only; (5) no mask intersection or subtraction, so a radial cannot be cut out of a linear; (6) **M3.1 does not close here**.

**M3.1a is complete — masks can now be authored on the canvas.** Plan B added `mask_tool`, draggable handles with guide lines, and a Masks panel section. 559 tests at the time. ⚠ **M3.1 still does not close until 3.1c's brush.**

- **No new mode, and the precedence rule is finer than the spec first said.** Handles appear when a mask is selected in the list; `geometry_mode` still wins while on. ⚠ But the spec's "otherwise the mask owns the drag" would have **killed panning**, because the non-geometry branch owns pan and zoom. The real rule: a drag belongs to the mask **only if it started on a handle**, which is what `active_mask_handle` records. Found by reading the canvas while writing the plan, not by shipping it broken.
- **⚠⚠ The plan's own sabotage step was a lie, and a subagent caught it.** `hit_testing_the_line_is_aspect_correct` used a **horizontal** axis with the probe point directly below it — where the perpendicular distance is purely vertical, so scaling x cannot change the answer. It passed with the aspect correction deleted: a test that named the bug it could not detect. Now a **vertical** axis with horizontally-offset probes, which genuinely discriminates (`Some(Line)` vs `None`). ⚠ Same lesson as the rotate/flip work: *a property test is only as good as the property*. Both times the test looked rigorous and checked nothing.
- **⚠ The aspect correction now appears in three places** — `LinearGradient::coverage`, `mask_tool::hit_test`, and the canvas's perpendicular guide lines. Each measures distance in **screen** space; normalised space is anisotropic and skews all three.
- **⚠ `mask_tool`'s `tol` is in frame heights; `crop_tool`'s is normalised.** Two siblings, same signature shape, different units — a tolerance copied across is wrong by the aspect ratio. Documented on `hit_test` after a review caught the silence.
- **Guide lines, not a coverage tint.** A faithful tint needs *three* quads because coverage is clamped flat either side of the ramp; two boundary lines say the same thing exactly and do not wash out the photograph. A tint behind a toggle stays available.
- **`selected_mask` is view state on `Session`, never in `Edits`** — a selection is not part of the photograph's description, so it must not reach the sidecar or transfer on a paste. Per-photo, so it resets on a switch for free.
- **⚠ The borrow trap fired exactly where the plan predicted.** `E0499`: the Masks section both wrote `settled = true` and called the `slider` closure, which independently captures `&mut settled`. Fixed by the codebase's established pattern — a local flag folded in after the closure closes — **not** `RefCell`.
- **⚠ `selection_after_delete` clears rather than moves.** Deleting the selected mask selects nothing, following `Selection::clamp`'s rule for the same reason: silently selecting a *different* mask means the next slider drag edits something the user never chose. A review also caught that deleting must clear `active_mask_handle`, or the canvas's early return leaves a stale handle and panning dead.
- **⚠ `let Mask::Linear(g) = m.mask` is irrefutable only while `Mask` has one variant.** 3.1b makes it a compile error — which is right: the overlay must then decide what to draw.
- **⚠⚠ Two bugs found in the first minutes of real use, and both are worth reading before touching the canvas.**
  - **The image did not update while dragging a handle** — it kept showing the mask's previous position until some *other* edit happened to request a frame, which is why moving a slider appeared to "fix" it. `draw_mask_overlay` mutated `Edits` but never called `s.request`. ⚠ The cause is an inherited false analogy: it was modelled on `draw_crop_overlay`, which needs no request *because* `preview_edits` strips the crop in geometry mode, so the overlay **is** the feedback. Masks are not stripped. Copying a structure without checking why its parts exist.
  - **The mask drifted with the view when zoomed**, following the visible part of the image while panning. The overlay mapped normalised coordinates onto `img_rect` — but once a region render is on, `img_rect` covers only the **rendered slice**, not the frame. Now placed against a `frame_rect` derived from the same whole-image origin the region placement uses, so the two agree by construction.
  - ⚠ **That second bug had three heads and only one was visible.** `aspect` was computed from `img_rect` too — a *region's* aspect, not the frame's — which silently corrupted hit-testing at high zoom; and the drag deltas were divided by the region's dimensions, so a handle dragged while zoomed moved by the wrong amount. Both would have surfaced later as "handles feel wrong when zoomed" with no obvious cause. **Every coordinate in that function is frame-relative now; `img_rect` survives only as the clip.**
- **Accepted caveats:** (1) linear gradients only; (2) no coverage tint; (3) **the panel's placement is provisional** — this is the concept that parked the layout work; (4) no mask reordering or renaming; (5) no keyboard nudging.

**Phase 3 started. M3.1a's headless half is done: a linear-gradient mask carrying per-pixel adjustments renders correctly, with no GUI involved.**

- **Phase 3 is four independent subsystems and was decomposed before anything was specced.** M3.1 masking first — everything recent was preparation for it, and it unblocks the parked layout work. ⚠ **M3.2** lens corrections needs lensfun, a new C dependency with the same build risk libraw was. ⚠ **M3.3** colour grading has a *hidden prerequisite*: real colour management does not exist — lcms2 emits one static sRGB profile and nothing else. **M3.4** virtual copies is the smallest and most independent.
- **⚠ M3.1 is itself sliced into three**: **3.1a** the architecture on one analytic mask (this), **3.1b** radial, **3.1c** brush. M3.1 **does not close until 3.1c** — its own "Done when" names dodge/burn with a brush.
- **⚠ And 3.1a is two plans.** This is plan A, headless. `pipeline` has zero GUI dependency by rule and the CLI is the permanent test harness, so the whole architecture — coordinate space, region-awareness, composition, blending, export — was proved against goldens and a real `.RAF` **before any canvas code exists**. Plan B is the UI. That also means a canvas bug can never be confused with a model bug.
- **A mask carries the per-pixel adjustments only** — temp/tint, exposure, contrast, highlights/shadows/whites/blacks, saturation/vibrance. ⚠ Not an arbitrary subset: it is exactly the set where "apply the stage, then blend by coverage" is correct *and* cheap, because each output pixel depends only on its own input.
